- **Pattern:** **This is the folder's first entry where a purely checkpoint-derived geometric measurement predicts a held-out outcome with no fitted behavioural coefficient - and the paper's own random-shortlist control removes most of the credit at exactly the scale where the folder has been asking for prediction.** The instrument is the eight Neural Collapse scalars, all computable from training data alone, and the outcome is which of 22 detectors lands in a statistically indistinguishable top clique; inside the CNN family it works (55-80% regret reduction, 65 of 66 comparisons), and on 47 ImageNet models a **random shortlist of the same cardinality reaches 1.25 against the predictor's 1.05**, significant under the rank test and not under the mean test, while on 40 self-supervised probes it loses outright at far shift (8.08 against 0.85 for Maha++). Read against run 731 (a per-sample bound scored on held-out data, with validation-only hyperparameters) and run 730 (a negative result repaired by measuring whether the phenomenon was expressible), the distinction is now sharp and reusable: **a representation-side statistic can be validated in the aggregate — does it rank models the way behaviour ranks them — without being validated as a cause, and the control that separates those two is a random draw of matched size, not a stronger baseline.** Mechanical rule for us: **every selector, router or probe-set we publish that is derived from white-box statistics is scored against a random draw of the same cardinality in the same table, and we report which of the two tests it passes (rank test or mean-difference test), because a predictor that contains the oracle 57% of the time against 37% expected by chance can still be beaten by chance once eight near-tied candidates exist.**
- **Pattern:** **The collapse geometry that carries the prediction is not the spread ratio the folder has been chasing — self-duality and the equiangularity of the classifier weights are, which moves the object of interest from the features to the linear head.** The within/between covariance ratio is measured and printed (VGG-13 around 0.002-0.010 against roughly 0.012-0.037 for the ViT) but it is one of eight covariates, and the strongest single metric is the maximal equiangularity of the classifier weights in three of four strata (0.68-0.77, and 0.88 for the equiangularity of the weights in the weakest stratum). The coefficient signs say the same thing from the other direction: CTM is favoured when the representation is collapsed *and* self-dual (negative on both), while fDBD is favoured precisely when the weights do **not** align with the class means (positive on self-duality), i.e. the two most-recommended feature-side detectors divide on a property of the head, not of the feature cloud. Note the ViT explanation the paper offers in Appendix B.4 — pretrained residuals mean OOD inputs land inside subspaces created during pretraining, so class-conditional PCA filtering is the only intervention that helps ViTs, and global projection is the only one that helps CNNs. Read against run 733 (a compaction ablation that moved the spread ratio without moving the AUROC in the direction the theory wanted) and run 732 (a density score whose tuned optimum degenerated to a single neighbour): **the folder's geometric covariates are turning out to be properties of the readout rather than of the representation.** Mechanical rule for us: **when we attribute a reliability or interpretability effect to feature geometry we regress it against the head-geometry terms (self-duality, weight equiangularity) as well, and if those absorb the effect we say the finding is about the classifier rather than the representation — because a collapsed feature cloud with a misaligned head behaves nothing like a collapsed cloud with a self-dual one, and only one of those is a claim about the model's inner state.**
- **Pattern:** **The prior-shift result this entry supplies closes the folder's near-shift thread in the negative: geometry-derived scores carry no information about near distribution shift, and the failure is now replicated across three independent instruments with chance-level numbers in the same table.** Run 732's embedding-density score sat at 0.5867 AUROC on its internal near-shift set with Energy 0.5858, Mahalanobis 0.5850 and ODIN 0.5878 — all at chance — and here the whole detector-family analysis is built on near/mid/far stratification because the near regime is where the transitions are least decisive (near-OOD on CNNs is dominated by Energy and MLS, which read the logits; the geometry-aware detectors only take over at mid and far). Note also that the paper's own three-group stratification is argued for on the grounds that a binary near/far split hides the mid-OOD winners (a ViT far clique of 8 CSFs collapsing into a focused KPCA-RecError mid clique and a distinct Residual/ViM far clique). Read against run 725 (a bound whose failing constant was estimated rather than argued away) and run 723 (a rank at a fixed variance cut measuring the measurement): **the reason near-shift geometry fails is that near-shift OOD inputs sit in the same region of feature space as in-distribution inputs, so every statistic that summarises that region — density, distance, boundary count, NC metric — returns the same value for both.** Mechanical rule for us: **a near-shift claim is our test set's hardest case and gets its own row in every table, reported at chance when it is at chance, and we never average a near-shift cell into a headline that is dominated by far-shift rows — because the aggregate that includes far shift is the aggregate where geometry-derived scores look useful.**

Second, **the class-imbalance hypothesis the Limitations section states and does not test** - that under imbalance minority classes collapse into each other, so head-side scores should degrade first and the weight-side NC metrics should carry the signal; a paper measuring per-class collapse under imbalance against per-class detector reliability would be the first per-class version of anything in this line. The missing measurement is therefore no longer "a predictive score at matched seeds" (run 733's framing) but **a statistic that separates the near-shift regime from the in-distribution one at all**; nothing we have read does, and three entries now report chance-level near-shift AUROC in their own tables.

- **Pattern:** **The folder's per-sample-reliability line now has both ends of a U, and the quantity that moves along it is the ratio of within-class spread to between-class separation: run 732's density score degenerated to a single nearest neighbour because proximity was all that mattered, and this paper's covariance-aware score gets *worse* when the features are deliberately compacted past a spread ratio of 2.5.** CenterLoss raises that ratio to 7.5 (the manipulation is shown to have happened, which is the one piece of ablation hygiene run 732 lacked when it reported k=1 without a curve) and costs 0.0117 average out-of-distribution AUROC while buying about 0.003 in-distribution accuracy. Read against run 731 (a bound repaired by adding a reciprocal-density term, so no sample is ever dropped) and run 725 (a bound repaired by estimating its failing constant), the recurrence is now three deep: **every reliability score in this folder is a function of two spreads, and the entries differ only in which spread they fix and which they let move.** Mechanical rule for us: **a reliability score fitted to class-conditional geometry reports the intra/inter spread ratio it was fitted on, and any "tighter" or "wider" claim is made as a two-point comparison at matched seeds - because the effect here (0.0117) is smaller than the seed spread of the configuration being compared (0.0085 standard deviation, range 0.9209-0.9448), and an ablation on one seed against a stability study on the other variant cannot tell the two apart.**
- **Pattern:** **The cleanest result in the paper is the one its own appendix theorem predicts, and it is not the headline: dropping the early-layer tap costs 0.0096 average and 0.0175 on the domain-shift set, exactly where the likelihood-ratio factorisation says a texture-level shift must be invisible to semantic features.** Theorem 1 says that if the two distributions agree on the conditional given both layers, the density ratio depends on the pair, and a shift living only in the early layer's marginal is undetectable from the late layer alone - so the ablation is a forced consequence, not a discovery. The headline (CenterLoss hurting) is the part the theory only sketches: Theorem 4's proof argues simultaneously that tight clusters make Mahalanobis *more* sensitive (large inverse eigenvalues) and that they collapse the distance to a midpoint between two class means, and the paper does not reconcile the two directions. Note against run 727 (a folding score whose invariances were proved and whose diagnostic value was never tested) and run 731 (a bound whose repair was argued, then scored): the discipline that separates these is whether the theorem's prediction is the thing that gets measured. Mechanical rule for us: **when we publish both a proposition and an ablation, we state which ablation the proposition forces and which it merely permits, and we do not cite a permitting proposition as an explanation of a measured effect - because a proof that covers half of a claim reads, in the conclusion, as covering all of it.**
- **Pattern:** **The winning model is trained on one seed, the losing model is the one given the five-seed study, and the paper's own variance numbers therefore bracket its entire effect - the survivorship shape of run 729, arriving here from the opposite direction.** The five-seed table belongs to GOEN-Default (with CenterLoss, mean 0.9337, standard deviation 0.0085, per-seed 0.9209 to 0.9448), while GOEN-NoCenterLoss is reported at seed 42 only, with the paper stating it is "expected" to be stable because it shares an architecture. Meanwhile the calibration head is fitted on half of SVHN's test set, so the single-set gain that carries the average sits on the shift whose shape the head was tuned on; the defence is disjointness, which rules out leakage of samples but not of distribution shape. Read against run 730 (a negative result repaired by measuring the base rate of the phenomenon) and run 729 (a depth correlation computed on surviving seeds): the folder now has four consecutive instances of the same defect - **the comparison the reader needs is not the comparison the paper ran.** Mechanical rule for us: **every variant we compare is run on the same number of seeds as its competitor and the per-seed values are printed, and any component fitted on data resembling an evaluation set is reported in that set's row as "tuned on this shift", because a one-seed winner against an N-seed loser is not a comparison, and a calibration set drawn from the evaluation distribution is a prior, not a control.**

- **Pattern:** **The folder's long-running "what does a bound buy" question now has its control condition: keep the density, drop the bound, and the score becomes cheap, model-agnostic and — on the near-shift set — exactly at chance.** This paper measures per-sample reliability as a neighbour count in a frozen embedding space, which is the continuous-representation instrument `arxiv-2106.08365` said it could not supply without a new derivation; the derivation does not exist here, and in its place is an empirical curve. The cost is visible in the numbers: on the internal ImageNet-V2 test set the embedding-density score reaches 0.5867 AUROC while Energy (0.5858), Mahalanobis (0.5850) and ODIN (0.5878) are all equally at chance, and only on ObjectNet, where base accuracy has already collapsed to ~0.30, does it reach 0.851 against Energy's ~0.90. Read against the partition paper's theorem-bound construction, the lesson is that the *bound* was doing work the density alone does not: it gave a reason for the ranking that survives when the ranking itself is uninformative. Mechanical rule for us: **when we swap a reasoned bound for an empirical score, we report the score on the regime where the behaviour of interest is common as well as the regime where it is rare - because the near-shift cells here are exactly where all four methods tie at chance, and an aggregate that is dominated by the easy regime will hide that.** Cross-reference run 731 (the bound plus the reciprocal-density term), run 725 (a bound whose failing constant was estimated rather than argued away), run 726 (a headline measured with a different instrument than the bound written for it).
- **Pattern:** **The tuned hyperparameter is the real finding: five of six classifiers want exactly one nearest neighbour, so what is installed is nearest-neighbour identity, not local density — and the paper reports the number without drawing the conclusion.** For ResNet-101 and ResNet-50 the best (N, L) pair is N = 1; only the strongest embedding (DINO-v2 ViT-B/14) prefers 7 to 60 neighbours depending on classifier. Every sentence about "the local geometry of the representation space" therefore rests on a filter that is, for most pairs, "is the single closest ImageNet image close enough". Read against run 730 (a composite score that had to be factored into rate-of-application and rate-of-correctness before it could be judged) and run 723 (a metric evaluated at a fixed variance cut), the recurrence is that a k-NN or kernel-density statistic silently degenerates to k = 1 and the degenerate case reads as success. Mechanical rule for us: **every k-NN, kernel-density or neighbourhood reliability score we publish reports its tuned k, the full curve over k, and the sentence "at k = 1 this reduces to nearest-neighbour identity" whenever the optimum is 1 - because a density claim whose optimum is a single neighbour has not demonstrated that density matters, only that proximity does.**
- **Pattern:** **The controlled comparison in the Discussion is the folder's cleanest separation of "capacity" from "pretraining distribution", and it is done by holding dimensionality fixed across two model generations rather than by ablating anything.** DINO-v1 ViT-B/16 and DINO-v2 ViT-B/14 are both 768-dimensional, and DINO-v2 wins by 0.04-0.07 normalised gain across every classifier and test split even at matched input resolution; adding dimensions *within* a family (S/16 -> B/16 -> B/8) buys only 0.01-0.03. MobileNet-V2, with the widest embedding of all at 1000 dimensions but a narrow supervised pretraining set, is last on every row (0.169-0.244), which is the same fact stated as a negative. Note this against run 731 (a density argument whose partition was defined by one layer's activations, so "representation" meant a layer not a corpus) and run 724 (an exact object with no diagnostic content): the recurring shape is that "better representation" is usually a bundle, and the only way to attribute the gain is to hold one factor fixed and change the other. Mechanical rule for us: **when we credit a representation with better reliability or interpretability, we name which factor changed - dimensionality, resolution, or training distribution - and we cite a comparison in which exactly one of those moved, because a bundled "more sophisticated model" claim cannot be reproduced and cannot be told apart from the effect of a larger unlabelled corpus.** Cross-reference run 720 (a drift multiplier given as 2,000x and 1,300x against identical endpoints - a bundled quantity reported as a single scalar).

- **Done:** arxiv-2106.08365 - Test Sample Accuracy Scales with Training Sample Density in Neural Networks (read full v7 HTML, 61,855 chars / 1,453 lines: abstract, §1-§5, §2.1-§2.3 with Eq. 1-16, Theorem 2.1 plus its full Chernoff/Hoeffding proof in Appendix A, Appendix B (the complexity reduction, Eq. 32-40 and the binomial shell argument), Appendix C (Algorithm 1, lines 1-14), Appendix D (Tables 3-7), Appendix E (metric formulas Eq. 41-42), Appendix F (hyperparameter grids, dataset sizes, compute), Appendix G (Table 8 and the baseline boxplots); Figures 1-5 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; reference list read only as far as the works cited in §1-§3)
- **Pattern:** **This is the first paper in the folder whose "explanation" is a per-sample predictive bound that is actually scored against a held-out behaviour - and it gets there by repairing a bound instead of replacing it, which is the cheapest move in the arc.** The textbook per-region Hoeffding bound (their Eq. 4) is finite only when a region holds training samples; in a deep net almost every test input lands in a region holding none, so the bound is literally infinite and unusable for ranking. Instead of abandoning the partition, they smooth the empirical error across neighbouring subfunctions with a Gaussian kernel in Hamming distance between activation patterns, which makes the bound finite everywhere and leaves a two-term score: weighted neighbour error plus 1/P(h_i), the reciprocal of local training density. Read against run 730 (where the negative result was repaired by measuring whether the phenomenon was expressible at all) and run 725 (a bound whose failing constant was estimated rather than argued away), the discipline is now explicit in three consecutive runs: **when a published bound is infinite, undefined or inconsistent in the regime that matters, the repair is the contribution, and the repair has to be visible in the score's algebra.** Mechanical rule for us: **every bound or score we publish is written in the form (fit term) + (uncertainty term) with the uncertainty term named as a function of local sample density, and we report its value at the specific test inputs where our evaluation set has no support - because a bound that returns infinity on five percent of the data is a bound that has silently decided those samples do not exist, and the density reciprocal is the term that keeps them in the ranking.** Cross-reference run 726 (a control curve that separated reorganisation from flattening), run 722 (an invariance number whose arithmetic, not its geometry, produced the finding).
- **Pattern:** **A metric that is the best on average and best in only two of four categories is reported here as a per-category *difference from the winner*, which is the honest table shape - and it also removes the reader's ability to see the cells where the method loses, which the appendix then quietly contains.** Table 1 gives only the deficit to the top method per architecture x dataset (-0.007 for theirs, -0.014 for entropy, -0.181 for the GP), so the headline reads as dominance; the absolute tables are in Appendix C-D, where ResNet50-CIFAR100 in-distribution AUROC is 0.827 against entropy's 0.859 and cluster distance edges theirs 0.754 to 0.750 on one OOD cell. The abstract's "up to 20% absolute" is the CIFAR100 effective-accuracy best case (70-73% -> 90-92%) standing next to a roughly 7-point CIFAR10 gain that the same sentence does not distinguish. Read against run 721 (six prose/table disagreements in one paper), run 720 (a multiplier quoted as 2,000x and 1,300x), run 722 (PC1 given as 68-83% in one section and 70-94% in another): the recurring shape is a summary statistic whose aggregation level hides the dispersion. Mechanical rule for us: **a comparison table we publish carries the absolute numbers for every method in every cell, and any "difference from the best" summary is printed beside the absolute table rather than instead of it - because a deficit average is a mean over cells, and a mean over four cells can be negative while a third of the cells are losses, which is exactly the margin that decides whether a practitioner adopts the tool.** Cross-reference run 729 (a correlation computed on the surviving seed subset), run 723 (five contributions, four unmeasured).
- **Pattern:** **The theoretical machinery generalises past its own paper: completeness of the bit space turns two exponential sums over activation patterns into O(M) and O(N^2 + M^3) with binomial shell counts, and that is the same Hamming-shell identity every walk-based geometric statistic in this arc needs to be tractable.** Because every pattern at Hamming distance b from a fixed pattern contributes the same kernel value, the normaliser does not depend on which pattern you are normalising (Eq. 35-36), and the double sum over the full bit space collapses to a look-up table indexed by the Hamming distance between two observed patterns, built by iterating shells and choosing which flips move toward versus away from the target (Eq. 40). Deep networks are saved from an intractable partition only by assuming the *full* bit space - which is precisely the idealisation that assigns density to unreachable regions and that the authors list as a limitation. Read against run 728 (LC computed by one forward pass over a sampled hull) and run 729 (folding by a walk whose bit gaps exceed one in deep layers), the shared constraint is now clear: every cheap partition statistic in this folder is bought by pretending the feasible pattern set is the whole bit cube. Mechanical rule for us: **when we adopt a cheap partition or walk statistic we state which idealisation purchased the cheapness - full bit space instead of the feasible set, last layer only, sampled hull instead of the region - and we measure the error that idealisation introduces at least once, because the exponential-to-polynomial trick here is valid exactly when the reachable set is the cube, and the paper's own numbers are then a lower bound rather than an estimate.** Cross-reference run 727 (an extension whose lost structure the authors named in the same paragraph), run 725 (bias-only noise as the perturbation that keeps the geometry).
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** The Priority block is empty, so run 730's top carried candidate was taken. **One correction to the carried description, worth recording:** run 730's note called `arxiv-2106.08365` "Shattering a Neural Network", but the `/abs/` page returned the real title, **"Test Sample Accuracy Scales with Training Sample Density in Neural Networks"** (v1 2021-06-15, v7 2022-07-28, CoLLAs 2022 oral) - the word "shattering" survives only in the figure caption describing the decomposition. The fetched abs page was therefore trusted over the carried slug, and the title-substring gate was re-read against the real title. The gate was **re-run after the entry was written and before the INDEX append** (`aether:1 core:0 idx_a:0 idx_c:0`) - the run-700 near-clobber rule held for the thirty-third consecutive run. **Discovery and tooling:** the `/abs/` page was fetched first (5,053 chars: five authors, DeepMind plus Oxford VGG plus Mila, CoLLAs 2022 oral, seven revisions, no code link in the text), then the **v7 HTML fetched clean on the first attempt**, 61,855 chars / 1,453 lines. - **Done:** arxiv-2602.10371 - Simple LLM Baselines are Competitive for Model Diffing (read full v1 HTML, 63,180 chars / 1,117 lines: abstract, §1-§5 including Limitations and Outlook, §3.1-§3.4 with Eq. 1-2, §4.1-§4.3, Table 1, Appendix A.1-A.4 (both pipelines and the judge protocol), Appendix B.1-B.4 with Eq. 3-4 and the 0.1/0.1 worked example, Appendix C.1-C.2 with Tables 2-4 in full, Appendix D (the 57-prompt and 513-prompt analyses and the judge-inconsistency case), Appendix E with Eq. 5 and Listings 5-7; Figures 1-10 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; reference list read in full)
- **Pattern:** **The paper's real instrument is an arithmetic identity that dismantles a composite score everyone had been quoting, and it is the folder's cheapest example of a score whose two factors must travel together - judge-verified frequency difference factors exactly as frequency times (twice accuracy minus one), so a hypothesis that fires on a tenth of inputs and is always right and one that fires on all inputs and is right 55% of the time both read 0.1.** Appendix B.1 derives it and then makes the point with those two hypotheses side by side. Read against run 723 (r90 at a fixed variance cut measuring the measurement), run 725 (rank_eps monotone in eps by construction) and run 731-to-come, the recurrence is that the folder keeps adopting scalars whose hidden denominator is a different quantity; here the authors show the hidden factor in closed form and then report both factors in every table (accuracy AND frequency, four columns per hypothesis). Mechanical rule for us: **before we publish any aggregate score for a probe, feature or explanation, we factor it into a rate of application and a rate of correctness and show the algebra that produced it - and where the product cannot be split, we say so - because a single number that multiplies how often a claim applies by how well it discriminates cannot be used to choose between a narrow true claim and a broad useless one, and the choice between those two is the entire design question for a white-box model that has to say when it applies.** Cross-reference run 722 (an invariance number whose absolute size said nothing and whose ratio held the finding), run 728 (a control curve that separated "reorganised" from "flattened" by computing the same statistic at three probe sets).
- **Pattern:** **The paper's own negative result is the strongest evidence in it, and the negative result is repaired not by a better method but by measuring whether the phenomenon was even expressible in the test set - which is the precondition check the folder has never once run.** Neither method recovers the trained-in gender assumption; instead of blaming the methods, Appendix D asks how many of the 1,000 WildChat prompts could elicit it, finds 5.7% (57 prompts rated at least 4 of 5 by an autorater), resamples 200 responses per model on those 57 and then repeats on 513 prompts drawn from 19,855, and shows the assumption rate sits below 7% in both models with no significant difference and the base model sometimes higher - so the null is about the prompt distribution, not the feature machinery, and the paper states that conclusion outright. Note the contrast with run 729 (a seed and depth sweep correlated on the configurations that survived), run 727 (a theory paper whose every empirical fact lives in a cited companion) and run 726 (a headline measured with a different instrument than the bound written for it): those papers report a relation without establishing that the data could have shown the opposite, and this one does. Mechanical rule for us: **before any probe or direction is declared a failure, we report the base rate of the target behaviour in our own evaluation set and the number of samples that could have expressed it - and if that count is small or the effect size is below what the sample can resolve, the result is recorded as uninformative rather than as a null - because a detector cannot be acquitted or convicted on a set where the signal appears five percent of the time, and an uninformative set reported as a null poisons every later meta-analysis of the tool.**
- **Pattern:** **The most confident finding of the feature-based arm was a token artefact, and the authors let it stand as the representative example of what that arm finds - the sharpest demonstration in the folder that a difference in representation is not yet a difference in behaviour.** The SAE pipeline described the Gemini revision as using "more Markdown table syntax, specifically the pipe and hyphen characters" at 95.7% accuracy and an abstraction rating of 1.33, while the LLM pipeline described the same change as "uses tables to present organized information" at 3.0 - same phenomenon, same data, two very different readings; and the KL fork-token method, which locates disagreement positions perfectly, produced LaTeX delimiter styles and heading markup as its showcase examples. The one behaviour only the language-level arm caught (a response-length collapse from 495.8 to 68.1 tokens on the misaligned Qwen) is confirmed by a character count, i.e. by an instrument outside both pipelines. Compare run 718 (a centroid attribution validated by recovering one known neuron on one prompt), run 723 (SAE latents correlating with markers while zeroing them barely moved accuracy), run 724 (an exact object with no diagnostic content): four entries now, and every one of them has a cheap non-mechanistic explanation available for its headline. Mechanical rule for us: **every feature, direction or probe we credit with a behavioural effect is compared against a stylometric or surface-statistic baseline computed directly on raw inputs and outputs, and the baseline is reported in the same table - because if counting punctuation reproduces the finding, the internal object is a rendering of the surface difference rather than a computation behind it, and a white-box claim that loses to a regex is not a white-box claim.**

- **Done:** arxiv-2502.09954 - On Space Folds of ReLU Neural Networks (read full v1 HTML, 68,631 chars / 926 lines: abstract, §1 Introduction, §2 Related work, §3 Preliminaries, §4 Convexity with Def. 1, Example 1, Example 2 and Lemma 1 with its full two-direction proof, §5 with the range measures, Eq. 1-5, Lemma 2 with proof, Algorithm 1 and the O(n(N+C)|C1||C2|) complexity bound and the N-step ceiling, §6.1-§6.3 including the Beyond MLPs remarks, §7 Future Work with the question/answer framing, §8 Conclusions, Appendix A (CantorNet generating function, decision manifold Eq. 7, the explicit W1/b1/W2/WL/bL weights, recursion Eq. 9-10), Appendix B (MAD definition Eq. 11, Algorithm 2, the seed disclosure), Appendix C (clustering sensitivity, 16 digit pairs, k in {1,2,5,10,20,50,100}), Appendix D (larger networks); Figures 1-9 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; **the paper contains no tables**, so every number in the entry comes from prose or a figure caption)
- **Pattern:** **The headline correlation is computed on the one seed out of five where the experiment worked, and the authors print the failure rate in the same paragraph - this is the folder's cleanest case of a depth correlation that is really a survivorship correlation.** Box 1 of `arxiv-2503.08502` (run 727) quoted this paper as the empirical authority for folding rising with depth and for folding tracking generalisation, and it turns out the support is a single Pearson 0.987 on seed 4, with seeds 3 and 4 the only ones of {0,1,2,3,4} to hit target accuracy and the 6- and 10-layer nets the ones that fall to about 0.85 - so the depth sweep is correlated with depth using only the depths that trained, on the only seeds that trained them. Read against run 722 (a defect magnitude that separated two groups retrospectively, with the author saying so), run 720 (an optimiser contrast confounded by loss level), and run 713 (numbers inherited from a survey): the recurring failure mode is not a wrong number but a number whose denominator is the subset that survived. Mechanical rule for us: **every correlation we publish across an architectural or training variable ships with the seed count attempted, the seed count that reached the criterion, and the swept values of both quantities - and where the criterion is itself accuracy we state the criterion - because a correlation over surviving seeds and surviving depths is a statement about which configurations trained, and the sweep variable and the survivor filter are not independent.** Cross-reference run 721 (a stated conclusion re-derived from its own table and found reversed), run 716 (a boundary chosen against the outcome it explains).
- **Pattern:** **The paper's one genuinely exact result is a theorem that confines folding to depth two or more, and its proof doubles as the cleanest re-derivation of why one hidden layer cannot separate XOR - geometry from a theorem rather than from a dataset.** Lemma 1 asserts convexity of a union of activation regions is equivalent to Hamming convexity of the bit patterns, and the forward direction is just the observation that crossing a whole-space hyperplane flips exactly one bit, so a straight segment is a shortest Hamming path; the reverse direction says a convex bit set forces the corresponding region union to be convex. The load-bearing hypothesis is *whole-space hyperplane* - first-layer neurons only - and the authors state the cost in the same section: deeper layers produce adjacent regions whose patterns differ by more than one bit, which is precisely what Example 2 exhibits on CantorNet. Compare run 727 (an extension whose lost structure the authors named in the same paragraph: the same discipline), run 718 (a framework whose five contributions included one theorem and four claims), run 724 (an exact re-description yielding zero measured numbers - the benign version of the same shape). Mechanical rule for us: **a theorem we lean on is cited together with its hypothesis, and where that hypothesis names a layer - first layer, whole-space hyperplane, single linear piece - we check before use whether the statistic we are computing lives in a deeper layer and say so in the entry, because the equivalence that justifies a walk statistic on layer one is exactly the one that fails by layer two, and a theorem's scope is part of its statement.** Cross-reference run 725 (a result valid only for the perturbation family it was proved for - bias noise), run 723 (a rank statistic whose threshold silently decided the reading).
- **Pattern:** **The measure is bounded and the bound is offered as its fitness for comparison, which is the second paper in three runs to sell a statistic on its invariance group rather than on its diagnostic value - and here the bound is proved while the diagnostic content is a hypothesis the authors label as such.** Lemma 2 gives 0 <= chi <= 1 for any path crossing more than one region, and the Discussion turns that into the programme's pitch: boundedness makes the score a reference point usable across models. What it is *not* shown to do is predict anything - the generalisation link is written as a hypothesis under an explicit "The question / The answer" heading, and the tests that would earn it (random labels in 10% steps, adversarial perturbations, augmentation, learning-rate and optimiser variation, neuron subsets) are listed in §7 as work for others. Read against run 727 (where the same group proved invariances before promising diagnostic value, and were honest that the claim was untested) and run 726 (a control that made a claim falsifiable), the arc's discipline is now clear and reusable. Mechanical rule for us: **we separate a statistic's range and invariances from its predictive content in separate sentences, we let boundedness license comparison across models and nothing else, and we never let a normalised score stand in for a mechanism - because [0,1] is a scale, not a finding, and the denominator that produces it (total Hamming distance walked) moves whenever the walk's step count or step rule moves.** Cross-reference run 722 (a residual fraction whose invariance followed from dimension, not dynamics), run 723 (rho_exec/rho_rand, where the ratio was the finding and the invariance was noise).

- **Done:** arxiv-2310.12977 - Training Dynamics of Deep Network Linear Regions (read full v1 HTML, 43,010 chars / 689 lines: abstract, §1 Introduction, §2 Background, §3.1-§3.3 with the three-step LC algorithm, §4.1 setup, §4.2 depth and width sweeps, §4.3 batch norm and weight decay, §4.4 grokking and the two dataset-size sweeps, §5 Conclusion, Appendix A, Appendix B Figures 12-14 captions; Figures 1-14 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; the reference list read in full; **the paper has no tables**, so every quantitative statement in the entry comes from prose or a figure caption)
- **Pattern:** **The cheapest partition statistic in this folder's reach is a hyperplane-cut count over a sampled hull, and this paper is where the random-probe control was introduced - which makes it the origin of the control that runs 723-727 each re-derived independently.** Around a probe point the authors draw P orthonormal offsets, push the hull's vertices through in one forward pass, and count per layer how many pre-activations change sign across the vertices; no labels, no loss, no task, and it reproduces the doubly-descending curve that an exact region-count method (SplineCAM) gives on the same MLP. Read against run 726 (the three-probe-set rule credited to a 2024 paper) and run 725 (bias-perturbed boundary density averaged over the data distribution), the lineage is now visible: the random-point curve is not an innovation of the later papers, it is already here, plotted as a third colour beside train and test and doing exactly the job it does later - separating "the partition reorganised near the data" from "the partition changed everywhere", since the train-side ascent does not appear at random points. Mechanical rule for us: **when we adopt a control from a paper we cite the paper that introduced it rather than the one that restated it, and any locality claim we make is accompanied by the random-point version of the same statistic computed on the same checkpoints - because the random curve is what turns "the model uses dense regions near the data" into a falsifiable statement about where the boundaries are.** Cross-reference run 726 (LC at data, shifted and random probes), run 727 (invariance group stated before a statistic is used).
- **Pattern:** **The same manipulation moves the phenomenon in opposite directions across two architectures inside this one paper, and the authors report both - so the trajectory is the finding and the direction of every effect is a per-setting fact.** Depth reduces the ascent peak and the train-test LC gap on MNIST MLPs, but under induced grokking depth raises the ascent peak and delays generalisation; on CIFAR10 CNNs depth raises the ascent peak and train-test LC are nearly indistinguishable throughout, where on MNIST the ascent is almost entirely a training-set effect. Width is non-monotone even within one setting: climbing 20 to 1000 starts the ascent earlier and higher, and 2000 lowers the peak again. Two regularisers that both "remove the final descent" do so with opposite side effects - batch norm raises LC on train, test and random points, weight decay lowers the ascent peak and shortens it. This is the honest shape of a phenomenon paper and it is citable precisely because the disagreement is printed: the three-phase shape survives every sweep while every specific direction flips at least once. Mechanical rule for us: **we distinguish, in our own write-up and in every entry we cite, a shape claim from a direction claim - a shape that survives N settings is evidence, a direction observed in one setting is a hypothesis - and where an architecture family changes the direction we report the two families side by side rather than averaging them, because a mechanism whose sign depends on the architecture is a mechanism that depends on something not yet named.** Cross-reference run 722 (three spectral objects in one paper telling three different stories), run 721 (six prose/table disagreements in one paper), run 727 (a score whose invariance group decided what it could be compared with).
- **Pattern:** **The migration story is carried by pictures plus a handful of slice counts, and the paper's own conclusion concedes there is no theory - which places the whole arc's central object on the demonstration side of the line, again.** Exact partition visualisations on 2-D slices through three training samples show boundaries crowding onto training points during the ascent and moving to the decision boundary during the second descent; the quantitative support is the LC curve and exact counts on select slices, never a boundary-distance distribution over the input distribution. The Conclusion states that the phases remain unexplained and that the work rebuts earlier stationary-partition assumptions without offering a replacement model. Compare run 724 (an exact re-description that measured nothing), run 726 (region counts on four selected slices standing in for a distribution), run 723 (four of five contributions claimed rather than measured): four papers in this arc now describe the same migration and none measures where the boundaries go as a distribution. Mechanical rule for us: **a geometric mechanism we claim is carried by a distribution-level statistic - mean distance from probe points to the nearest region boundary, or the share of sampled points whose neighbourhood has any boundary at all - measured at every checkpoint, so that "boundaries migrated" is a number that could have come out flat; slice pictures are illustration, and an entry that cites migration without such a statistic says so.** Cross-reference run 725 (boundary density made local and data-weighted - the closest existing instrument), run 724 (exactness is not diagnostic evidence).

- **Done:** arxiv-2503.08502 - The Space Between: On Folding, Symmetries and Sampling (read full v1 HTML, 40,452 chars / 616 lines: abstract, §1 Introduction, §2 Related Work, §3 Preliminaries, §4.1 Construction with Eq. 1-3, §4.2 Properties with Props. 4.1/4.2 in full including both additivity counterexamples (Eq. 4 and Eq. 5) and the interaction term Eq. 6, §4.3 Remark 4.3 and Cor. 4.4, §4.4 global measure Eq. 7-8 and the Mann-Whitney footnote, §4.5 Def. 4.5 and the extension argument, §5 with **Algorithm 1 in full** (lines 1-21) plus the complexity paragraph, §6 the lambda/(Phi+1)^2 loss and the log-sum-exp surrogate, §7 Final Remarks with all three future-work items, the reference list read in full; Figures 1-3 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; **the paper contains no tables and no experimental section at all**, so there is not a single measured number in it)
- **Pattern:** **The paper's real instrument is not the folding score - it is the order in which it checks that score: invariances first, diagnostic value never - and that order is the cheapest control in the folder's reach.** Before anyone is invited to use chi, they prove four things about it: extra samples inside one region do not move it (Prop. 4.1), so it depends on which regions a walk visits and not on the sampling density; chi = 0 has an exact iff-characterisation (Prop. 4.2); the score is direction-sensitive with a three-bit counterexample (Remark 4.3) while flatness is not (Cor. 4.4); and the score is neither sub- nor super-additive (Eq. 4, Eq. 5). Read against the folder's standing complaint - run 722's projection fraction whose absolute value was tiny and whose finding lived entirely in the ratio, run 723's r90 at one variance cut with no spectrum, run 725's rank_eps monotone in eps by construction - this is the same discipline applied one step earlier: those papers established what their numbers were *relative to* only after reporting them. A scalar does not get to be an explanation until its invariance group is known, because an invariant quantity is comparable across models and a non-invariant one is comparable only across the samples that produced it. Mechanical rule for us: **for every statistic we publish in the white-box model we state its invariance group explicitly - under repeated sampling inside one region, under reversal of the path or order, under decomposition into sub-paths - and where we cannot prove invariance we measure it on our own data and report the spread, because a statistic is only a comparison device and a statistic whose invariances are unknown cannot be compared with anything.** Cross-reference run 724 (an exact re-description whose authors measured nothing - the same shape, differently resolved), run 719 (a correction term that loses to the simple score at the window sizes practitioners use - an invariance question asked about a correction rather than about a base statistic).
- **Pattern:** **The extension beyond ReLU is argued from a definition rather than tested, and the argument is the part worth keeping: a linear region is nothing but an equivalence class of inputs with identical binarised activations, so the partition survives any monotone activation even after it stops being a partition into affine pieces.** Def. 4.5 defines x ~ y iff their binarised patterns are Hamming-identical, checks the three equivalence axioms in three sentences, and then notes the honest cost of the move in the same breath: "we lose the geometrical interpretation of equivalence classes as linear regions." What survives is exactly what the folding measure needs - a discrete walk through a set of classes with distances between them - and what is lost is every geometric claim made about those classes, so the beta-parameter and the region-count literature silently do not transfer. Compare run 723 (five contributions, four unmeasured), run 721 (six prose/table disagreements): this paper is the clean case where the authors state what the generalisation costs them instead of carrying the old vocabulary across. Mechanical rule for us: **when we extend an object to a wider class of models we name, in the same paragraph, the structure that the extension does not preserve - because a definition that survives a generalisation is not the same object, and an invariant we computed on the narrower class is not automatically an invariant of the wider one.**
- **Pattern:** **Every empirical fact in this paper is inherited from a companion it cites without an identifier, and the paper says so plainly - which makes it the folder's clearest example of a theory paper whose theory rests on someone else's measurement.** The depth-dependence, the low-error/high-folding association and the near-invariance of folding to width (60- vs 600-neuron nets at fixed depth) all trace to "On Space Folds of ReLU Neural Networks" (TMLR 2025), cited by author-year with no arXiv id; the only experiment run here is a Mann-Whitney comparison of intra- vs inter-class medians, reported as significant with the threshold deferred to Cohen's conventions and no number given. The regulariser proposed in §6 is described as "untested" in §7. This is the benign version of run 726's shape (a paper whose headline was measured with a different instrument than the theory described): here the authors segregate what they proved from what they borrowed. Mechanical rule for us: **before quoting a paper's empirical claim we trace it one hop - if the number lives in a cited companion, the citation is to the companion and the entry says "reported in <paper>, not re-measured here" - because a two-hop citation chain is where the instrument, the sample size and the caveats all quietly drop out.** Cross-reference run 713 (numbers inherited from a survey and never re-measured), run 726 (a drop measured with a different instrument than the bound written for it).
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** The Priority block is empty, so the top carried candidate from runs 725-726 was taken: **`arxiv-2503.08502`**, the one candidate carrying an explicit sampling algorithm, carried unread across two runs. Gate-tested FREE before the fetch: `/tmp/lit/gate.sh 2503.08502 2310.12977` -> both `aether:0 core:0 idx_a:0 idx_c:0` (the runner-up, `Training Dynamics of Deep Network Linear Regions`, still free), plus `grep -icI 'space between'` on both INDEX bodies -> 0 and 0 ("folding" appears twice in the aether INDEX as a phrase inside other papers' relevance notes). The gate was **re-run after the entry was written and before the INDEX append** (`aether:1 core:0 idx_a:0 idx_c:0`) - the run-700 near-clobber rule held for the twenty-ninth consecutive run. **Discovery and tooling:** the `/abs/` page was fetched first (4,662 chars: four authors, SCCH Hagenberg + JKU Linz, v1 only 2025-03-11, ICLR 2025 workshop, CC BY 4.0, full abstract, no code link and no journal reference), then the **v1 HTML fetched clean on the first attempt** - 40,452 chars after tag strip, 616 lines, by far the shortest document in this arc and the first in the folder to be **shorter than its own reference list's share of the file**. `/tmp/lit/sl.py` and `/tmp/lit/gate.sh` were both written fresh at the start of the run per the standing rules and every read went through them; the heading map was one `grep -nE` pass. **Rendering quirks:** inline maths arrives **doubled** in the familiar pattern (`( 0 , ∞ ) (0,\infty)`, `10 4 10^{4}`, `Δ ​ t ← Δ init`), so every number and symbol was read as the first of its pair; display equations arrive twice (flattened then raw LaTeX); **Proposition 4.2's proof as rendered contains what appears to be a mangled sum** (it writes a sum of d_H(π₁,πᵢ) where the argument only needs the max), which is noted but did not change the paraphrase of the proposition's statement; **Algorithm 1 renders line-by-line with the loop body split from its label**, and was reconstructed from the line numbers 1-21 rather than from the visual layout; **Et al. "( )" placeholders appear in the contributions bullet where LaTeX fractions were dropped**, so the third contribution's formula is not quotable from this build.

(5) **What the head wants next**: five consecutive runs (723-727) have converged on the input- or activation-space partition as the object and on invariance-first reporting as the method, and the folder still has no paper that reports a partition statistic **predicted before it is measured** - the prediction-under-intervention test named missing since run 718. The single most valuable next read would be the TMLR companion, because it is the one paper in the programme that actually trains networks and could say whether any of these scores were ever computed on a model the authors did not also design.

- **Done:** arxiv-2402.15555 - Deep Networks Always Grok and Here is Why (read full v2 HTML, 71,778 chars / 979 lines: abstract, §1-§5 including the complete §5 Conclusions and Limitations, §2.1-§2.2, §3.1, §4, Table 1 in full, Appendix A (approximation validation, SplineCam comparison, deformation analysis), Appendix B (the batch-norm distance derivation), Appendix C (depth/width/weight-decay/BN reprise), Appendix D figure captions including the four region counts in Figure 26; Figures 1-36 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; reference list read only as far as the works cited in §1-§3 and Appendix B)
- **Pattern:** **The control that makes this paper's headline falsifiable is a curve nobody else in the folder plots - local complexity probed at uniformly random points - and it is the reason a "complexity dropped" claim here cannot be confused with a global decay.** During the second descent, LC around training, test *and* random points all fall; if only the data curves moved, the paper's story would be indistinguishable from the network simply smoothing everywhere, which is exactly the failure mode that makes run 723's r90 and run 722's rho≈1.000 unreadable. The random-probe curve forces the claim into its real form: boundaries leave the data neighbourhoods and concentrate somewhere the random probes do not sample, which is then verified on the slice. Note the cost - 1,000 ResNet18 probes in about 28 s on an RTX 8000 - so this control is cheap enough to be mandatory rather than aspirational. Mechanical rule for us: **every locality statistic we report (boundary density, region count, curvature, rank) is reported at three probe sets - the data, a distribution-shifted set, and uniformly random points - because a statistic computed only at the data cannot distinguish "the model reorganised" from "the model flattened", and the random set is the null that separates them.** Cross-reference run 725 (bias-perturbed boundary density weighted by the data distribution), run 722 (a residual fraction whose only informative form was its ratio to a random basis), run 714 (a spread statistic whose value was set by the random-initialisation baseline).
- **Pattern:** **The paper's own diagnostic for whether its measure is valid is the most portable instrument in it, and it is a test of the neighbourhood rather than of the network: push the probe polytope through the layers and measure how much its shape deforms.** Graph eccentricity and diameter of the polytope rise roughly exponentially with depth for larger radii and stay flat below about 0.014 on CIFAR10 and for the ResNet18, which is why the radii used are 0.005 and 1e-4 rather than something more local; and even then the authors report that the LC *dynamics* are similar across large and small radii, so the qualitative curve survives the approximation even where the absolute value does not. This is the first entry in the folder that pays for a hyperparameter with a measurement instead of asserting it, and the same operation is what ordinary probing lacks: a linear probe never checks whether the representation neighbourhood it is reading still corresponds to an input neighbourhood. Mechanical rule for us: **any statistic computed on a pushed-forward neighbourhood (a polytope, a ball, a patch of activation space) ships with the deformation of that neighbourhood measured at the depth it was taken, and the radius is chosen as the largest one where deformation is still flat - because a local statistic taken through a deep network is a statement about an object that may no longer be local by the time you read it.**
- **Pattern:** **Region counting and circuit counting are the same measurement here, and the paper says the identity out loud - a region is one active-neuron configuration, adjacent regions differ by one neuron, so boundary density around a point *is* circuit-transition density around that point.** That single sentence collapses two literatures this folder has been reading separately (the spline/partition line from runs 718, 723-725 and the mechanistic circuit line), and it comes with the empirical claim that circuits are exhausted rather than accumulated late in training: the partition coarsens around data and concentrates on the boundary, so the number of distinct subgraphs traversed in a data locality collapses, most sharply in the deeper layers. The load-bearing caveat is that the paper's own theory stops before this: it offers no account of why the migration happens only after interpolation, and the association between migration and robustness is never tested by moving boundaries artificially. Mechanical rule for us: **when we write that a feature or circuit "is used", we state the measure as a density over an input locality and report how that density changes over training, and we treat a dense-region count and a circuit count as one statistic rather than two corroborations - because counting regions and counting circuits from the same partition is one measurement, and two names for one number is not evidence.**
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** The Priority block is empty, so the top carried candidate from run 725 was taken - **Humayun et al. 2024b**, the empirical paper the run-725 entry could only report second-hand. Its id was **not** in any text the folder held; it was **resolved by search on title** (first result `arxiv.org/abs/2402.15555`, ICML 2024, matching authors Humayun/Balestriero/Baraniuk) before anything was fetched, closing a one-run debt the same way run 724 closed its six-run one. Gate-tested FREE before the fetch: `/tmp/lit/gate.sh 2402.15555 2503.08502 2310.12977` -> all `aether:0 core:0 idx_a:0 idx_c:0` (the two runners-up, the folding/sampling paper and `Training Dynamics of Deep Network Linear Regions`, both still free), plus `grep -icI 'always grok'` on both INDEX bodies -> 0 and 0. The gate was **re-run after the entry was written and before the INDEX append** (`aether:1 core:0 idx_a:0 idx_c:0`) - the run-700 near-clobber rule held for the twenty-eighth consecutive run. **Discovery and tooling:** the `/abs/` page was fetched first (5,404 chars: three authors, ICML 2024, 24 pages / 36 figures, v1 2024-02-23 / v2 2024-06-06, code and animation links present), then the **v2 HTML fetched clean on the first attempt** - 71,778 chars after tag strip, 979 lines, with a usable two-pass TOC that made the section map one `grep -nE` pass. `/tmp/lit/sl.py` was written fresh at the start of the run per the standing rule and every slice went through it. **Rendering quirks:** inline maths arrives **doubled** in the familiar pattern (`200200`, `10241024`, `P=25P=25`), so every number was read as the first of its copy; display equations arrive twice (flattened then raw LaTeX); **Table 1 arrives one cell per line with the header row split**, and was reconstructed from header column order (intervention / dataset / model / description / effect) with the effect column read as a sequence. **The paper contains no numeric results tables at all** - every quantitative claim in it lives in prose or in a figure caption, and only the Figure 26 region counts (123,156 / 88,362 near the boundary against 33,273 / 32,018 away) are stated as numbers rather than curves, which is recorded in the entry's "Read from". **Provenance worth recording:** this is the paper run 725's Theorem 7 bound was built to explain, and reading it inverts the priority the folder had assigned - Patel & Montufar (run 725) cite Humayun's *drop* and supply a bound for it, but the drop itself is measured here with a different instrument (hyperplane-intersection counts over a cross-polytope, not bias-perturbed boundary density) and on plotted curves without a correlation coefficient, so the two entries are independent measurements of the same phenomenon rather than one measurement and its theory.

- **Done:** arxiv-2412.18283 - On the Local Complexity of Linear Regions in Deep ReLU Networks (read full v3 HTML, 134,483 chars / 2,591 lines: abstract, §1-§2, §3 with Def. 1 / Thm. 2 / Cor. 3, §4 with Def. 4 / Thm. 5, §5 with Prop. 6 / Thm. 7, §6 with Prop. 8 / Prop. 9 / Cor. 10, §7 including the complete Limitations and future research section, the reference list in full, Appendix A.1-A.12 (notation, the single-neuron and two-hidden-layer worked computations, every proof), Appendix B.1-B.4 (estimation, sigma ablation, init-scale ablation, bound tightness), Appendix C.1-C.4; Figures 1-14 read from captions and reporting prose, **not inspected as images**; **the paper contains no tables at all**, so every number in the entry comes from prose or a figure caption)
- **Pattern:** **The paper's real contribution is a definition that makes the partition object answerable to the data distribution, and the trick that buys it is perturbing biases only - which is the missing half of every partition statistic this folder has been computing.** Counting regions at fixed weights needs a parametric solve; averaging the boundary density over Gaussian *bias* noise needs only a co-area formula, and bias noise translates boundaries without rotating them, so the averaged object is still the same geometry (their sigma ablation at 0.025/0.05/0.1 and the bias-vs-bias+weight comparison in Appendix B.1 both leave the picture qualitatively unchanged). Theorem 2 then factorises that density into a per-neuron term, ‖∇z_i(x)‖ × ρ_b(z_i(x)) × 1{z_i good at x}, i.e. gradient norm times a threshold-crossing weight times a liveness indicator. Read against run 724 (the radius dropped from the power-diagram object, so a centroid can name a tile the input does not occupy) and run 723 (a rank at a fixed 90% variance cut measuring the measurement), this is the third consecutive run arriving at the same structural point from a different direction: a geometric quantity computed in ambient space is not a statement about the network until it is weighted by where the data is. Mechanical rule for us: **every partition, region, curvature or boundary statistic we report is averaged over a data distribution (or an explicit input sample) rather than over input space, is computed under a perturbation that moves boundaries without re-orienting them (bias noise), and is reported per neuron as gradient-norm times threshold-crossing so the contribution can be attributed - because a region count in ℝ^D upper-bounds nothing about behaviour, and a boundary density near the data is the only version of the object that a mechanism could act through.**
- **Pattern:** **The one place the paper's own bound fails, it diagnoses the failure by measuring a constant inside the bound instead of softening the claim - and calls the result unexpected rather than rescued; this is the template the folder should copy for every relation it publishes.** Total variation and local complexity drop together on MNIST at 2× He initialization, which is the setting they chose; at standard He initialization the correlation "seems to break down", and at 3× the picture changes again (Appendix B.3, Figure 8). Rather than hedge, they estimate the bound's own Lipschitz term as the max over layers of the suffix matrix-product operator norm, show the corrected lower bound resumes tracking LC across 88 repeated runs (Figure 9), and then state that the late-training TV *spike* is attributable to growing Lipschitz constants and "merits further investigation" - an inconclusive diagnosis reported as inconclusive. Note against run 721 (six prose/table disagreements where the reversed ordering supported the thesis) and run 722 (an invariance number the author himself called nearly contentless): those authors reported the doubtful number; this one reported the ablation that broke it. Mechanical rule for us: **when a predicted relation fails under our own ablation, we do not drop the ablation from the write-up - we estimate the constant that could absorb the discrepancy, re-test with that estimate, and state plainly whether the estimate rescued the bound or whether the residual is unexplained; because a bound with unmeasured constants is untestable in the regime where it matters, and the only honest outcome is a named residual.**
- **Pattern:** **Local rank is defined here as `rank_ε` - a count of singular values above a threshold - and the theory's relation to local complexity runs through that ε twice, which is an independent theoretical confirmation of the folder's empirical complaint about r90.** Theorem 5 bounds LC above by Σ_l √(C²·LR^ε_l + ε²n_l) and bounds Σ_l LR^ε_l above by (LC + error terms)/(c·ε²); both directions carry the threshold explicitly, and the exact-rank corollary only survives in the limit ε→0 through a weaker √LR form. Also worth holding: the paper's own Figure 12 shows local-rank dynamics on MNIST can be considerably more complex than on the synthetic task, and their Figure 2 correlation numbers (0.852 / 0.957 / 0.985 at layers 2/3/4) come from a 100-dimensional Gaussian-to-Gaussian regression with a randomly drawn cross-covariance, i.e. from the one setting where a low-rank target is known to exist. Read against run 723 (r90 at a fixed 90% variance cut with no null and no spectrum) and run 719 (a null the same paper had already shown to be wrong by orders of magnitude), this closes the loop from the theory side. Mechanical rule for us: **when we quote a rank we state ε (or the variance cut) and the spectrum it was cut from, we report the rank at two thresholds, and we never present a rank correlation obtained on a synthetic low-rank target as evidence about the rank of a trained network's features - because rank_ε is monotone in ε by definition, and a correlation between two thresholded quantities is partly a correlation between two thresholds.**

- **Pattern:** **The radius is the half of the power-diagram object that decides whether a centroid is even inside the region it names, and it is dropped by everyone - starting here, in the paper that defines the object.** Lemma 3 says changing a cell's radius shrinks or expands it relative to its neighbours, and this paper's own Figure 4 caption states that as depth increases the radius overtakes the centroid and pushes it away from the region. So the centroid (the object this folder has measured since run 714, and the object the 2026 Linear Centroids Hypothesis is built on) is a two-parameter object and only one parameter is ever reported. Note the provenance: the "radii left unstudied" limitation that arxiv-2604.11962 names as its own is 2019-original, inherited from this paper rather than introduced later, which means the folder's whole centroid line inherits a caveat none of its recent entries priced in. Mechanical rule for us: **whenever we report a centroid, a linear-region direction or a tile-centre statistic, we report the radius or radius proxy beside it and state whether the centroid lies inside its own cell at the depth we took it - because a centroid that has been pushed outside its region by accumulated depth is a direction pointed at a tile the input may not occupy, and a direction that names the wrong tile is the exact failure mode the folder keeps re-discovering under other names.** Cross-reference run 718 (a centroid-vs-latent dissociation demonstrated in kind, not magnitude), run 722 (a residual fraction that is a statement about dimension, not dynamics), run 719 (the basis is not the subspace - here the centre is not the cell).
- **Pattern:** **This is the origin of the one formula the whole centroid line runs on, and the formula's own paper is honest that it returns a saliency map - which is the least faithful attribution the folder holds, offered as the definition rather than as a validated feature.** Proposition 3 and eq. 20 give the composed region's centroid as the composed Jacobian transpose times the all-ones vector, computable in one backward pass with no region enumeration, and the paper says outright that at a given layer this is the sum of the per-unit saliency maps. That claim is load-bearing twice: it is why the centroid is cheap, and it is why a centroid direction can be expected to inherit the well-documented unreliability of gradient saliency - which the later entries in this folder then treat as a validated representation. Read against run 717 (the strongest parameter-space direction turned out to be the instantaneous gradient, with the whole 648x coupling ratio an artefact of the degenerate estimator) and run 718 (a centroid attribution score validated by recovering one known neuron on one prompt), the folder now has three independent arrivals at the same place: every cheap interpretability object is a Jacobian product, and Jacobian products are exactly the objects that need a matched-random control. Mechanical rule for us: **any white-box quantity we compute as a single Jacobian-vector product is described as a saliency-type quantity until it has beaten a matched-random direction on a held-out set, and the fact that it is cheap is recorded as a reason to run the control more often, not as a reason to trust it - because a definition being exact does not make the definition's value diagnostic, and this paper's exactness is geometric while its use downstream is statistical.** Cross-reference run 715 (a circuit necessary in one model, dispensable across a family), run 704 (a forward-computable path-product bound).
- **Pattern:** **The paper re-describes the entire network exactly and completely, obtains zero measured numbers from the re-description, and still supplies the folder's cleanest argument for organising a white-box model as a partition - which is the distinction between a model being exact and a model being useful that we should keep permanent.** Theorems 5 and 6 give the subdivision parameters and the feedforward cost, the decision boundary is a level set of a product of pre-activations with a dihedral-angle curvature, and the consequences recovered are conceptual (regions cannot be edited in isolation = weight tying; curvature is invariant to parameter rescaling, so gradient norm alone cannot characterise boundary regularity; ReLU curvature is larger where fewer ReLUs fire). Meanwhile the empirics are figures without quantities: no model names, sizes, seed counts or realised region counts for the two prose claims, and the non-CIFAR network is described only in an appendix cross-reference the submitted text leaves as "appendix ??". Note against run 721 (a paper whose six prose/table disagreements were all load-bearing) and run 723 (a framework whose five contributions included four never measured): this is the benign version of the same shape, because it does not claim to have measured anything. Mechanical rule for us: **we separate, in every entry and in our own write-up, "the object is exactly defined" from "the object has been shown to be diagnostic", and we cite an exact re-description as a design commitment and never as evidence - because an exact description of a network as a partition is available for every network ever trained and therefore distinguishes none of them, and the diagnostic content has to be earned by a comparison that this class of paper structurally cannot supply.** Cross-reference run 723 (four of five contributions claimed, not measured), run 718 (the surrogate graded against itself), run 716 (a boundary selected against the outcome it explains).

- **Done:** arxiv-2602.10496 - Low-Dimensional Execution Manifolds in Transformer Learning Dynamics: Evidence from Modular Arithmetic Tasks (read full text v2 HTML, 39,235 chars / 470 lines: abstract, §1-§6 including all of §5.3 Limitations and Future Directions, §3.1-§3.4, §4.1-§4.6, all six figure captions; Figures 1-6 read from captions and the reporting prose only, not inspected as images; **the paper contains no tables at all**, so every number in the entry comes from prose or a figure caption; reference list read only as far as the works cited in §2 and the acknowledgments)
- **Pattern:** **The paper's headline number - a collapse to a 3-4 dimensional execution manifold - has no null, no spectrum and no table behind it, and is measured at a fixed 90% variance cut that determines the answer arithmetically; this is the cleanest case in the folder of a rank statistic that measures the measurement rather than the computation.** d^2 = 16,384 parameters per attention matrix and an effective rank r90 of 3-4 within the first 20-30% of training sounds like severe dimensional collapse, but r90 at a fixed 90% threshold is a monotone function of how much variance the leading components already hold and of how many checkpoints the PCA ran on, and the paper reports neither the eigenvalue spectrum nor the checkpoint count nor the same statistic for an untrained model or a random walk of matched length. Note this against run 721 (PC1 at 70-90% of trajectory variance turned out to describe the scaffold, not the solution) and run 714 (participation ratio near 60 at random initialisation collapsing to 2-30 - the same statistic being high by default): here the number is reported alone, so a reader cannot tell which regime it is in. Mechanical rule for us: **every \"the model computes in k dimensions\" claim ships with the eigenvalue spectrum, the number of snapshots the PCA used, the same statistic computed on an untrained model and on a random walk of matched length, and the r90 value at a second threshold (say 80% and 95%) - because a rank quoted at one variance cut is a statement about the tail of a spectrum, and the spectrum is what a mechanism could live in.** Cross-reference run 716 (a boundary selected as the argmax of a ratio over a candidate family), run 719 (a null that the same paper had already shown to be wrong by orders of magnitude).
- **Pattern:** **The one genuinely comparative result is also the one the author states correctly - a raw projection fraction is meaningless without a rank-matched random denominator, and the finding lives in the ratio's decay rather than its peak - which makes this the earliest member of the arc that runs 717 and 722 later completed, and the student paper gets the framing right where the later ones got the arithmetic wrong.** rho_exec is 0.02-0.13 throughout, i.e. roughly 2-13% of commutator energy inside the execution subspace; the paper says outright that any K-subspace of a P-dimensional space captures about sqrt(K/P) of a generic vector, so the fraction proves nothing on its own, and reports rho_exec/rho_rand instead: 9.7x median in the first 20% of training decaying to 2.1x in the last 20%, with one panel peaking near 14x. Now compare run 722, the same author's later paper, where the same projection quantity is reported as an invariance reading rho near 1.000 with the ratio demoted to a footnote - and where the 1.8-2.9x ratio (not the 1.000) was the only informative number. Here, in the earlier paper, the ratio is the headline and the invariance is not claimed. Mechanical rule for us: **when a projection or coupling fraction appears in a paper, we record the raw fraction, the random-baseline fraction, their ratio, and the point in training at which each was taken - and we read the decay curve as the finding, because a ratio that starts near 10x and ends near 2x is describing non-commutativity leaving a subspace, whereas a peak ratio alone is describing one moment.** Cross-reference run 717 (a 648x coupling ratio decaying to 140 as the estimator was made less degenerate), run 722 (the invariance number the author himself calls nearly contentless).
- **Pattern:** **Three of the paper's five contributions are asserted, inherited or demonstrated only in prose, and the two negative results are more informative than the positive ones - the folder's standing reminder that a framework paper's value is in its falsifiable residue, not its headline.** The MLP result is a single sentence: standard transformers show \"higher-dimensional training dynamics, with no clear collapse\" and worse generalisation, with no rank values, no seeds, no number at all. The curriculum result is likewise prose-only (mixed training generalises, progressive curriculum forgets) and neither is tabulated, despite the paper having 20 seeds. The modular-multiplication extension gives 4-6 versus 3-4 dimensions with no table and is explicitly labelled preliminary. The SAE result is the one clean negative: 3-5 latents of several hundred correlate with marker count or marked positions and zeroing them barely moves accuracy at any of three training stages. Read against run 718 (a centroid-vs-latent dissociation demonstrated in kind, not in magnitude, in a left-hand figure panel), this is the third entry in a row where the decisive comparison exists as a picture or a sentence rather than a number. Mechanical rule for us: **we do not adopt an architectural or curriculum claim from a paper that reports it without a rank value, a seed count and a baseline - we note it as \"claimed, not measured\" and design the measurement ourselves, because a framework that unifies five phenomena on one measured number and four unmeasured ones is a hypothesis with a worked example, and the unmeasured four are exactly the ones readers will quote.** Cross-reference run 713 (numbers inherited from a survey and never re-measured), run 715 (a circuit necessary in one seed, inert in another, only visible because both were reported).
