- **Done:** arxiv-2602.16746 - Low-Dimensional and Transversely Curved Optimization Dynamics in Grokking (read full text v3 HTML, 86,506 chars / 1,250 lines: abstract, §1-§8 including §6.7 Limitations and Open Problems and the Reproducibility note, §2.1-§2.3, §3.1-§3.5, §4.1-§4.7.4, §5.1-§5.5, Tables 1-3 reconstructed from header column order and cross-checked against prose restatements, appendix Figure 22-26 captions; Figures 1-26 read from captions and reporting prose, not inspected as images; reference list read only as far as resolving works cited in §§1, 4, 5 and 6)
- **Pattern:** **The paper's headline invariance number is arithmetically almost contentless, and the author is the one who says so - which makes this the folder's cleanest worked example of why a residual fraction in a huge space needs a matched-random denominator before it means anything.** rho ≈ 1.000 "within numerical precision" across all 36 conditions sounds like a strong claim that curvature never deflects the trajectory; in fact both the execution-basis and the random-basis projections of the commutator are under 0.05 of the vector's norm, because a 24-dimensional subspace of 290,000 dimensions captures almost nothing from a generic vector, and the invariance follows from the arithmetic rather than from the geometry. The only informative statistic is the ratio between them - 1.8-2.9x in favour of the learned basis, stable when K is moved to 16 and 32 - and even that ratio is measured on the small parallel component. Note this against runs 717, 721 and 714, each of which reported a projection or variance-share number whose absolute value was tiny and where the finding lived entirely in a comparison: this paper is the version where the author states the reasoning explicitly. Mechanical rule for us: **any projection, variance-share or residual fraction we report ships with the matched-random denominator *and* the absolute share printed beside it, and when the absolute share is below a few percent the sentence is written about the ratio and never about the invariance - because in a parameter space of hundreds of thousands of dimensions every low-dimensional subspace is approximately orthogonal to everything, and \"the trajectory stayed inside its subspace\" can be a statement about dimension rather than about dynamics.** Cross-reference run 717 (a coupling ratio that decayed 648x as the estimator was made less degenerate - the same percentage-of-a-big-space problem met from the other side), run 721 (PC1's 70-90% share describing the scaffold, not the solution), run 714 (a spread statistic whose value was set by the random-initialisation baseline).
- **Pattern:** **Necessity and sufficiency came out asymmetric in a way that should be the default expectation for direction-level interventions: suppressing the component along a learned direction damaged behaviour with a clean monotone dose-response, while injecting perturbation along the curvature direction at up to 500x the gradient step norm did nothing at all.** The suppression curve is the good kind of causal evidence - 20-25% delay at strength 0.75 in all four operations, 0/12 successes at 1.0, and a rank-matched random projection that is inert at intermediate strengths - and the kick experiment is a true null: 27/27 runs generalise at the same step whether the kicks are commutator-aligned or random-orthogonal. The author reads this correctly as curvature being a *signature* of a barrier rather than a manipulable cause. Two bounds have to travel with the result and one is easy to miss: at full strength the random projection also kills grokking, so specificity is established only in the partial-strength regime where the intervention is a nudge rather than a cage. Mechanical rule for us: **we separately report whether removing a direction hurts and whether adding to it helps, and we treat \"removal hurts, addition is inert\" as the signature of a route the model must travel rather than a variable of the mechanism - and any specificity claim from a graded intervention states the strength range over which the control is inert, because at the hard end of the sweep every subspace is fatal and the comparison collapses.** Cross-reference run 721 (subspace-deletion dose-response with a non-monotone recovery point at ten times the dose), run 715 (a control matched on the axis of the claim), run 717 (a diagnostic subspace that matched a random one at its own rank).
- **Pattern:** **The one number in the paper that separates the two groups never appears alone at the same strength in both groups, and three different spectral objects in the same paper tell three different stories - which is the folder's most compact illustration that \"the leading structure\" is not a stable object across a decomposition.** Defect magnitude reaches 500-2000x baseline for the grokking operations against 30-50x for the two that never generalise, with zero overlap in totals but complete overlap early in training, so the separation is retrospective - the author says so plainly and refuses to sell it as a predictor. Meanwhile the update-Gram gap ratio is *higher* in memorising runs (2.83 against 1.40), the weight-SVD sub-leading gap declines 15-111x in 12/12 grokking runs against 1/12 controls, and the displacement-PCA PC1 that the whole manifold story rests on is quoted as 68-83% in §4.1 and as 70-94% in §5.4 for the same quantity. The author's explanation for the inversion - memorisation gradients are themselves strongly rank-1 - is plausible and untested. Mechanical rule for us: **before calling any direction \"the leading one\" we name which matrix it is leading in (updates, displacements, or the learned operator) and report the other two, because the same model at the same step ranks them differently and the one that matters to a mechanism is not the one that dominates the spectrum; and a quantity quoted at two different values inside one paper is recorded with both.** Cross-reference run 720 (a drift multiplier given as 2,000x and 1,300x against identical endpoints), run 721 (six prose/table disagreements in one 42-page paper), run 716 (a boundary selected against the outcome it explains, at a different scale).

- **Done:** arxiv-2602.18523 - The Geometry of Multi-Task Grokking: Transverse Instability, Superposition, and Weight Decay Phase Structure (read full text v3 HTML, 107,484 chars / 2,835 lines: abstract, §1-§13 including §12.6 Limitations and the reproducibility note, all of §9.5's Gram-matrix section, all fifteen tables reconstructed from header column order and cross-checked against prose, and the figure captions - Figures 1-34 not inspected as images; reference list read only as far as naming works cited in §1 and §12)
- **Pattern:** **The scaffold-solution split is the folder's clearest statement that a variance-ordered decomposition ranks the thing you do not want first - and it makes every PCA/SVD summary we have been quoting suspect by construction.** PC1's share of trajectory variance is 70-90% in these runs and reconstruction with the leading components alone is at chance; the capability appears only once k* directions (5-10 dual-task, 4-6 tri-task) are included, and the accuracy flips from chance at k*-1 to 99%+ at k* with nothing in between. The author's reading is that the dominant direction is the memorisation path, not the destination. Note this against runs 714 and 717-720, all of which read a dominant trajectory direction or a top-PC concentration as a substantive finding: here the same statistic is measured against a functional criterion and turns out to be a description of the scaffold. Mechanical rule for us: **no rank, concentration or cumulative-variance figure is ever reported alone - the rank we report is the smallest k at which a *reconstruction or behavioural test* passes, and PC1's share is printed beside it as the scaffold fraction, because a decomposition ordered by variance places the path first and the solution last, and only a functional criterion can tell which is which.** Cross-reference run 721's own §10.2 ("variance explained is not a reliable proxy for functional relevance"), run 714 (participation ratio is high at random initialisation and collapses to a specialism - the same measurement inverted), run 720 (drift direction integrated by momentum is 25x better aligned with accumulated updates than with any single gradient - an ordering that depends on which object you sum).
- **Pattern:** **This is the causal experiment the last four runs kept naming as missing - delete a fraction of the update's component orthogonal to a measured subspace, sweep the dose, and match a random-subspace control - and its most valuable output is the part that does not fit.** The dose-response is monotone and steep: 1% deletion costs about 29% more steps, 7% costs about 203%, 10% and above fails outright, while a random projection at 0.25 strength does nothing at all, so the effect is about *which* directions were removed and not about the size of the perturbation. Then at 50% deletion the dual-task model groks again at a 163% delay and the tri-task model never does. The author reads this as redundant centre manifolds - overparameterisation supplying alternative escape routes - and concedes the alternative (implicit low-rank reconfiguration) cannot be separated with one seed. Mechanical rule for us: **a subspace deletion sweep reports its non-monotonic points as findings rather than smoothing them into a threshold, and every threshold we publish carries the recovery behaviour beyond it, because a "critical deletion fraction" that becomes passable again at ten times the dose is evidence about escape routes and not about an information bottleneck - and only the full curve distinguishes a wall from a door you cannot reach.** Cross-reference run 715 (a mitigation that relocates rather than removes), run 713 (ablation damage is architecture-dependent because families differ in what else can carry the behaviour), run 710's carried question (direction-level intervention during training with a predicted effect size - this paper is the closest thing in the folder to that).
- **Pattern:** **A control with the right sign and the wrong magnitude is the most instructive kind: the no-decay runs have negative curvature comparable to grokking runs (about -50 against -63) and never generalise, so the instrument that grades severity inside the regime has no relation to the regime itself.** The same paper's own phase diagram shows grok time and curvature depth covarying across lambda and then breaking at lambda = 0 - the point where the covariate reads "nearly as extreme" and the outcome is absent. Read against run 719's honest admission (24/24 grokking runs with decay against 1/24 without, with no direct manipulation of the mediating quantity) and run 720's confounded SGD control, this is the third run in a row where the causal quantity is a covariate of weight decay rather than a manipulated variable. Mechanical rule for us: **when we claim a quantity is the mechanism rather than a correlate, we exhibit at least one condition where the quantity is held high and the outcome is absent - and if we cannot, we write "covaries with" and never "drives", because every measurement of a training-time quantity is measured under some regulariser, and the regulariser is the thing that moved.** Cross-reference run 719 (weight decay as a hyperparameter contrast dressed as a mechanism test), run 720 (optimiser substitution confounded by loss level), run 712 (a percentile threshold stable across training while a maximum swings two orders of magnitude).
- **Pattern:** **Six places in a 42-page paper with fifteen tables where prose and table disagree, every one of them load-bearing - and the file's own summary is the one that is wrong.** The tri-task condition is add+mul+sq everywhere except Table 13's label, "add + mul + sub"; §12.5 says k* rises from about 5 dual-task to about 7 tri-task while §10.5 and the Conclusion say the reverse and use the reverse as evidence for constraint-induced compression; §11.3 gives dual-task a wider k* range than tri-task in the same sentence arguing tri-task is more tightly packed; the tri-task lead fraction is called monotonic when it runs 82% then 77% then 97%; the orthogonal complement is quoted as about 1% of trajectory variance in one figure caption and as under 1% of *gradient* variance in §8.3 where the causal claim rests; and one tri-task probe table's internal spread (35,000 at 3% against 36,200 at 2%) sits unmotivated inside an otherwise monotone dose-response. Mechanical rule for us: **before quoting a paper's stated conclusion number we re-derive it from the table it came from, and where a paper gives the same quantity in two directions we record both and cite neither - because the errors that survive into abstracts are the ones where a reversed ordering supports a thesis, and a coherent narrative is exactly what makes them invisible.** Cross-reference run 720 (the drift multiplier given as 2,000x and 1,300x against identical endpoints), run 713 (a survey quoting its own study count as three different numbers in three sections), run 716 (an argmax boundary selected against the outcome it explains).

- **Done:** arxiv-2602.23696 - Optimizer-Induced Low-Dimensional Drift and Transverse Dynamics in Transformer Training (read full text v3 HTML, 72,522 chars / 1,950 lines: abstract, §1-§11 including all subsections, §10.7 scope and limitations, §11.1-§11.5 methods, Tables 1-6 reconstructed from header column order and cross-checked against prose restatements, Figures 1-4 read from captions and the prose that reports them but not inspected as images, reference list read in full - 16 entries, four of them the author's own 2026 preprints with no arXiv ids in the text)
- **Pattern:** **This paper supplies the causal control the whole spectral-edge arc (runs 710, 716-719) was missing, and it answers the question in the direction nobody expected - the structure is in the *optimiser*, not the loss surface - but the control is confounded at exactly the point that matters, and the confound is invisible in the headline.** Swap AdamW for SGD family with model, data, seed and schedule fixed: SGD without momentum never trains at all (validation above 8, probe at chance), both momentum variants crawl to validation near 5.1 with probe accuracy under 0.015, and geometrically every SGD run is essentially collinear - 100% of drift in one component, `k95 = 1` - while AdamW over the same window shows 61.5% and `k95 = 9`. Read that as "the edge is a property of the optimiser" and you have read a null result about training as a null result about geometry. The paper's own matched-loss attempt does not fix it: the AdamW windows it compares against sit at validation below 3.4 versus the SGD plateau at 5.1, and the author states outright that AdamW's pass through 5.2 happens inside the first 200 steps where there are no checkpoints. Mechanical rule for us: **an optimiser-substitution experiment counts as evidence about geometry only if the substitutes reach the control's loss inside the analysis window; where they do not, the result is reported as "this optimiser did not produce this training outcome" and never as "this geometry requires this optimiser" - because a trajectory that ends 3.5 nats away from the control is a different experiment, not a different optimiser on the same experiment.** Cross-reference run 719 (a hyperparameter contrast - weight decay - dressed as a mechanism test, with the same structure one level up), run 713 (numbers inherited from a survey and never re-measured here; these numbers are the author's own), run 715 (necessity in one model, dispensability across a family).
- **Pattern:** **The single most portable instrument in the whole arc is a pair of alignment numbers taken on the same direction, and the gap between them is 25x - which means every gradient-derived attribution we have been quoting is measuring an object the optimiser is not moving along.** Per-batch gradients align with the dominant drift direction at about 0.008-0.012, near the isotropic floor; the 200-step cumulative parameter change aligns at 0.15-0.32, twenty to thirty times higher; and the *signed* alignment flips from negative to positive near step 5100, exactly where the global direction rotates. The author's mechanism is simple and checkable - momentum integrates a weak persistent sign bias, and second-moment normalisation downscales high-variance coordinates so the low-variance coherent ones survive - and the β-2 sweep grades it smoothly: concentration 68.8% to 52.5%, early alignment 0.37 to 0.27, probe accuracy 0.951 to 0.005 as the second-moment estimator is turned off. Note what this does to run 717's finding that the strongest parameter direction *is* the instantaneous gradient: that was measured inside a rolling-SVD estimator at W=1, and here the same family of question asked with accumulated updates instead of a smoothed gradient gives the opposite ordering. Mechanical rule for us: **whenever we say a direction "matters" we report its alignment with instantaneous gradients and with accumulated updates side by side, and we trust the accumulated number - because the object that shapes a trajectory is twenty times better aligned with the sum of the steps than with any one of them, and a single-batch attribution inherits all of the noise and none of the coherence.** Cross-reference run 717 (the 648x coupling ratio at W=1 falling to 140 at W=40 - the degenerate-estimator limit of exactly this measurement), run 704 (a path-product bound computed forward from weights rather than from gradients).
- **Pattern:** **A slow direction can be simultaneously the most stable thing in the model and the least rotatable, and the paper measures both with the same spectrum - this is the cleanest statement the folder has of why stability and controllability are different axes.** Adjacent rolling windows align at a mean cosine of 0.80 and never drop below 0.70, so the direction is locally smooth; yet early and late phase directions agree at only 0.32, a rotation of about 71 degrees, and the trajectory passes through a dead zone near step 3500 where it aligns 0.02 with the early direction and 0.16 with the late one. Meanwhile curvature along the direction rises roughly 3,000x over training and the anisotropy ratio spikes to 12.4 at step 4800, precisely when the rotation is under way. Then reheating from the final checkpoint drives probe accuracy back to 0.782 (above the training peak of 0.777) in a thousand steps and it decays to 0.279 by two thousand: the capability was never erased, it became expensive to reach. Mechanical rule for us: **a direction we report as "stable" ships with its rotation over the full run and its curvature at the checkpoints we used, and any claim that a model "cannot" do something is checked by a re-heating or re-weighting probe rather than by an ablation - because a locally smooth tangent can accumulate 71 degrees of global rotation, and a capability suppressed by stiffness is still present, just unreachable at the learning rate you were using.** Cross-reference run 715 (a mitigation that relocates the sink rather than deleting it), run 712 (a percentile threshold stable across training while a maximum swings two orders of magnitude - here stability and drifting are separated by which window you take), run 719 (a stability coefficient that loses to its own simplest component at the window sizes practitioners use).
- **Pattern:** **Two arithmetic slips sit in the prose around numbers that the paper repeats elsewhere correctly, and both are in the load-bearing ablation - which is the folder's standing reminder that a repeated number is not a checked number.** The β-2 ablation's drift multiplier is given as about 2,000x in the mechanism section and the discussion but about 1,300x in the ablation section, against identical endpoints (120 to 1.6x10^5, which is about 1,300x), and the SGD control's "AdamW with the same architecture achieves PC1 around 81%" is quoted against a Table 4 whose AdamW row reads 61.5 - two windows, correctly, but the prose does not say so where the comparison is made. Compare run 713 (a survey giving its own study count as "over 210", "over 200" and "over 180" in three sections) and run 716 (an argmax boundary selected against the outcome it explains). Mechanical rule for us: **any number that appears more than once in our own writing is generated once and pasted, never re-typed, and any pair of numbers we compare in a sentence is accompanied by the window or configuration each was measured in - because the errors that survive review are not the ones that are wrong, they are the ones that are nearly right in two places at once.** Cross-reference run 709 (a gate that survives its own routing being deleted is the gate that was the mechanism).

- **Done:** arxiv-2603.28964 - Spectral Edge Dynamics: An Analytical-Empirical Study of Phase Transitions in Neural Network Training (read full text v3 HTML, 63 pages: abstract, Part I §1-§3 the objects and the three assumptions, Part II §4-§5 noise vacuity and the Hessian/Krylov argument, Part III §6-§7 the definition of k* and its null, Part IV §8-§10 order parameter, Davis–Kahan and level crossings, Part V §11-§15 the flow equations, Part VI §16-§18 stability coefficient, loss decomposition and the Gap Maximality Principle, Part VII-VIII §19-§29 the full system and all eight empirical tests including the causal test, Part IX §30-§44 connections, open problems, closing remark, and the reference list; all ten tables reconstructed from header column order and cross-checked against prose; Figures 2-5 not inspected as images)
- **Pattern:** **This is the analytical parent of run 710's empirical paper, and reading it dissolves the distinction that four earlier runs were groping for: the "spectral edge" and the optimiser's coherent direction are not the same object, they are the *subspace* and its *basis*.** Run 717 found that a parameter-space diagnostic subspace was interchangeable with random directions of the same rank; run 718 found the general geometric object (a centroid of an affine tile) that the whole programme is a probe of; this paper supplies the skeleton both were standing inside - a dominant block of k* modes separated from a subdominant block by one gap, with the entire dynamic content in the gap size. The load-bearing move is that "signal versus noise" is the wrong axis in this regime. With p/W around ten million the classical Baik–Ben Arous–Péché threshold sits orders of magnitude below the weakest mode, so every mode is signal and the interesting boundary is between committed and wandering signal. For us this is the correct frame for reading weights: our problem is not detecting directions, it is telling the directions the network has settled on from the ones it is merely passing through. Mechanical rule for us: **report any spectrum-derived statement with its aspect ratio and with the position index at which it was taken, and never quote a signal/noise separation that came from an isotropic null without also reporting the empirical spread of the noise modes in the same window** - because when the null is off by two orders of magnitude, as run 710 already measured, the null is describing a different problem.
- **Pattern:** **The paper's most portable instrument is a stability coefficient computed by splitting the window in half, and its most instructive result is that the instrument loses to its own simplest component at the window sizes a practitioner would actually use.** The coefficient is one minus the ratio of window perturbation to squared gap, floored at zero, and it needs no Hessians and no gradients - just the spectrum. It is what makes "how reliably is this direction held" a number. But the causal sweep (§28) reports rank correlation 0.21 and 0.42 at windows of 10 and 20 checkpoints against 0.76 and 0.72 for the plain gradient product, crossing over only at 30 (0.75 versus 0.63) and 40 (0.68 versus 0.35). The paper reads the crossover as the minimum window for resolving subdominant modes and that is probably right; the consequence it does not draw is that most published trajectory analyses use windows of ten to twenty. Compare run 711 (a percentile statistic stable where a maximum swung two orders of magnitude, visible only because both were tabulated) and run 718 (a surrogate graded by recovering a single known answer). Mechanical rule for us: **a correction term added to a simple score is reported with the simple score's value alongside it at every setting we actually used, and the claim "the correction helps" is only made above the crossover point where it demonstrably does - because a two-parameter refinement that wins at a window size nobody chose a priori is a refinement tuned by the sweep.**
- **Pattern:** **The control design here is a hyperparameter contrast dressed as a mechanism test, and the paper is honest enough in its phrasing that the gap shows.** The headline is 24/24 grokking runs with weight decay against 1/24 without, with the sub-leading gap compressing 33-43x at the grokking step. But weight decay simultaneously changes the spectrum, the loss trajectory, the weight norms and whether the model memorises at all; no run manipulates the gap directly. The author's own wording - weight decay as a driver "by which" the dynamics are associated with generalisation - concedes the association. Meanwhile the one test that does intervene, removing a signal direction from the updates and comparing against the predicted per-direction loss change (§28), is the one that came back window-limited, and the one prediction that came back null (gap-flow sign correlation r = +0.08, R² = 0.006) is excused by the equation having three terms rather than retested. Cross-reference run 713 (numbers inherited rather than measured) and run 715 (a pathway real in one seed, inert in another, only visible because both were reported). Mechanical rule for us: **when a treatment variable is a hyperparameter, the intermediate variable it is supposed to work through is named as an association until a direction-level intervention is run, and that intervention is the one we design first - because a co-occurrence between a spectrum and a behaviour is compatible with both of them following from a third cause that the hyperparameter also moves.**
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** The top carried candidate from run 718 - `arxiv-2603.28964`, Xu's Spectral Edge Dynamics - was **free**, with all four checks printed before any fetch: `arxiv-2603.28964 aether:free core:free idx_a:0 idx_c:0` (and the second carried candidate, `arxiv-2602.23696`, likewise still free). The gate was **re-run after the entry was written and before the INDEX append** (`aether:1 core:0 idx_a:1 idx_c:0`, i.e. the key now appears exactly once, in this folder only) - the run-700 near-clobber rule held for the twenty-first consecutive run. **Discovery and tooling:** the `/abs/` page was fetched first (5,353 chars: single author, gmail contact, no institution, v1 2026-03-30, v3 2026-05-07, 63 pages / 5 figures, CC BY 4.0, full abstract), then the full v3 text was fetched once - **215,561 chars after tag strip, 6,159 lines** (the report line printed 228,726 for the raw file). Slicing went through `/tmp/lit/sl.py` per the standing rule; the heading map was built in one pass over the file with a regex on bare `N.N Title` lines, giving a clean 44-section map, and every subsequent read was a line range. **Rendering quirks:** as in runs 711-718 every inline math fragment arrives **doubled** (`R¯k≈\bar{R}_{k}\approx`, `k∗k^{*}`), so every number was read as the first of its pair; **display equations arrive twice** (flattened mathtext then raw LaTeX); and **table cells arrive one per line with the header row split across lines**, so all ten tables were reconstructed from header column order and confirmed against the prose restatement of the same figures (e.g. the 8.0x/22.9x minima, the 0.818/0.234/0.000 hierarchy, the 0.21/0.42/0.75/0.68 causal sweep, the 24/24 versus 1/24 grokking contrast). **Figures 2-5 were not inspected as images** and are used only where the prose states the same content; the entry says so in "Read from". **Provenance worth recording:** this is the paper run 717's reference list named as the source of the W=20/K=3 defaults that its own paper inherits without ablating - and indeed here W is swept explicitly in the causal test only, while the main panel fixes W = 10. - **Done:** arxiv-2604.11962 - The Linear Centroids Hypothesis: How Deep Network Features Represent Data (read full text v1: abstract, §1-§5 including Discussion, Limitations, Impact Statement and Acknowledgments; Appendices A-H including the formal theory, the polygon/GELU replications, MNIST, the Platonic-representation check and the compute table; displayed maths read from the TeX pass; Figures 1-17 read from captions and reporting prose, not inspected as images; reference list read in full)
- **Pattern:** **This is the first entry in the folder that relocates the feature from the layer's output space to its *input* space and gets a falsifiable prediction out of the move - and the prediction is a dissociation, which is worth more to us than any accuracy gain it reports.** Because a centroid is the Jacobian of a sub-component summed over output dimensions (`J^T·1`), it describes what the local affine map *does*, whereas a latent activation describes only *where the input landed*. The paper's load-bearing experiment follows directly: inject colour into FashionMNIST at a swept correlation with the label, then probe colour linearly from latents and from centroids. Latents stay linearly decodable for colour even at random correlation; centroids are decodable only to the extent the label depends on colour. That is a claimed criterion for spuriousness - present-but-unused versus used - and it is the first operational test this folder holds for the distinction every "spurious feature" warning since run 690 has gestured at. Caveat that must travel with it: the evidence is a left-hand panel of a figure with no tabulated effect size and no seed spread, so the dissociation is demonstrated in kind, not in magnitude. Mechanical rule for us: **for every feature we report, we report both readings - the representation-side one (is it linearly decodable from activations?) and the action-side one (does removing or perturbing it move the output map?) - and we call a feature spurious only when the first is positive and the second is flat, because a direction being present is not the same as a direction being used, and only a pair of readings can separate them.** Cross-reference run 717 (a diagnostic subspace interchangeable with random of the same rank - the same "presence is not use" failure, met from the causal side), run 695 (a construction baseline making a score interpretable).
- **Pattern:** **An attribution score computed as a mean relative displacement over a neighbourhood is an intervention-shaped quantity obtained without running the intervention, and this folder now has the clean statement of what such a surrogate buys and what it does not.** Ablating one neuron and measuring the relative change in the sample's centroid, then averaging over a 256-point ball of radius 0.25 around the input embedding, ranks the previously published "an"-neuron of GPT2-Large in the top 99.8th percentile of its layer at layer 31 - a filter that avoids sweeping every neuron, arrived at from one prompt. Two properties are checked (low variance across draws, stable percentile across neighbourhood sizes) and one is not: the score is never validated against the ablation it stands for. So the claim "this ranking matches what ablation would find" rests on one recovered known answer, i.e. a test on the training case. Compare run 696 (a shuffled-community null beating every activation-only score) and run 717 (a 648x coupling ratio falling monotonically to 140 as the estimator is made less trivial) - both are cases where a surrogate was graded against itself. Mechanical rule for us: **any cheap surrogate for an expensive intervention (a gradient-based attribution, a centroid displacement, a Jacobian norm) is graded by rank correlation against the expensive intervention on a held-out set of at least dozens of cases, reported as a scatter and a Spearman value, and a surrogate validated only by recovering a single known answer is described as "consistent with" rather than "predicts".** Cross-reference run 694 (the one-forward-pass screen, first column only), run 696 (the shuffled null), run 717 (the degenerate-estimator limit).
- **Pattern:** **A paper whose every result is the shape of a plot is asking the reader to accept a method on one reading of its geometry - and the giveaway here is that the alternative hypothesis is criticised for exactly that.** The LRH is faulted for directions that are "linear even when the feature is spurious", yet the LCH evidence is PCA scatters, probe-accuracy curves, an 8-neighbour image grid and saliency images; the numbers that would settle it (probe accuracy, firing frequency, the cosine-similarity distributions whose bimodality at 0.4 carries the Platonic-representation argument) live only inside panels. The one quantity actually tabulated is compute cost - and it is not uniformly small: 8.7s versus 7.8s for DINO extraction, 881 versus 771 on MNIST, but 2329 versus 470 (about 5x) for the Llama-3.1-8B probes. Compare run 712 (a percentile threshold stable where a maximum swings two orders of magnitude, because both were tabulated) and run 715 (a circuit necessary in one seed, inert in another, only visible because both seeds were reported). Mechanical rule for us: **when we adopt a representation for our own probes we adopt it with a tabulated head-to-head on held-out data across seeds and a stated cost multiplier, and a new object that is cheaper on one experiment and 5x dearer on another is reported as a cost/benefit curve rather than a drop-in replacement - because "negligible in the grand scheme" is a claim about a budget, and budgets are where surrogates quietly stop being affordable exactly on the large models we care about.**

The "an"-neuron source is a 2023 single-post finding (Clement & Joseph), not a peer-reviewed paper, which weakens the one quantitative recovery claim.

- **Pattern:** **A feature-formation subspace can be maximally diagnostic and simultaneously non-causal and interchangeable with a random subspace of the same rank - and this paper is the folder's cleanest worked demonstration because both numbers come from the same intervention.** Keeping only a rank-3 attention subspace accelerates grokking 2.20x when the subspace is the gradient-SED basis and 2.36x when it is a freshly drawn random 3-subspace, statistically indistinguishable across three seeds and two operations; removing either one does nothing (1.02x and 1.00x). So the object that carries a 100x+ centroid-coupling ratio buys exactly the same speedup as a random subspace of its rank. This is one level sharper than the folder's previous warnings (run 715: necessary in one seed, inert in another; run 716: one implementation among several) because here the diagnostic and the null are evaluated in a single experiment on one model. Mechanical rule for us: **every time we report that a recovered direction, feature or subspace "matters", we also report the same intervention with a random subspace of the same dimension at the same site, and if the two are statistically indistinguishable we write "rank, not direction" - because a coupling ratio, however large and however reproducible, is a property of the measurement geometry, and only the matched-random intervention tells us whether the direction is doing the work.** Cross-reference run 714 (a circuit necessary in s42 and inert in s271), run 716 (every sink component individually dispensable), run 707 (magnitude and direction separated by three ratios).
- **Pattern:** **Most of a spectral coupling number can be inherited from shared Jacobians rather than from feature structure, and the window sweep here is the folder's best instrument for detecting it.** At W=1 the rolling SVD reduces to the normalised instantaneous gradient, and the coupling ratio is 648x; the author states outright that this is near-tautological because the parameter gradient and the embedding-space centroid are built from the same Jacobians, so any perturbation along the gradient moves centroids mechanically. The ratio then falls monotonically with the window - 648 at W=1, 617 at W=2, 394 at W=5, 212 at W=20, 140 at W=40 - which makes the canonical setting a chosen averaging timescale, not a discovery. The paper is honest about this (it says the "spectral edge" contribution is not load-bearing in single-task) and it is the reason its multitask result is the real one. Mechanical rule for us: **any sensitivity or attribution number produced by perturbing a model along a quantity derived from its own gradients is reported together with the degenerate-parameter limit of that estimate (W=1, instantaneous, single-batch) and the ratio between them - because a chain-rule-implied response is not evidence of a privileged feature direction, and only the decay curve as the estimator is made less trivial tells us how much of the number survives.** Cross-reference run 716 (the Marchenko-Pastur null rejected by 229-576x yet still used as the detection floor), run 706 (variance versus magnitude separated by a norm-matched control), run 708 (a scale fixed at initialisation decides what the fit finds later).
- **Pattern:** **Aggregating gradients across objectives before asking which directions matter to one objective destroys the answer, and this is the folder's first evidence that the destruction is attributable rather than mysterious.** In the shared-encoder multitask model the aggregated AdamW update gives a coupling ratio of 0.1-0.9, i.e. worse than random, for all four operations while all four still grok past 0.99 test accuracy; per-operation gradients recover 20-45x for every operation, larger than the strongest single-task number. The author lists four contaminants (momentum, per-coordinate adaptive scaling, weight decay drift, inter-task cancellation) and shows the fourth is the one that pushes the estimate across the random baseline. This matters for us because our own probes are computed on a model trained on a mixture, and every activation or parameter statistic we take is a statistic of the aggregate. Mechanical rule for us: **whenever a probe or spectral statistic is computed on weights trained on more than one objective, we say which objective's gradient the recovered direction is aligned with and report the per-objective version alongside the aggregate - because an aggregate direction is a compromise between objectives, and a compromise direction can sit below a random baseline for every one of them.** Cross-reference run 713 (ablation damage is architecture-dependent, the same quantity read differently by family), run 716 (one implementation across architectures), run 704 (the path-product bound, a forward-computable surrogate).

This was the right read and it closed the programme loop opened in runs 714 and 716: run 714 measured activation-space spectral structure, run 716 the same structure in parameter deltas, and this paper is the bridge, because it perturbs parameter space and reads the response in centroid (representation) space, so the question "is the spectral edge the same coherent direction the centroid lives on" is finally asked directly - and the answer is that the strongest parameter-space direction is the *instantaneous gradient*, that the temporal window only denoises it, and that the resulting subspace is diagnostically excellent and causally interchangeable with noise. **Discovery and tooling:** the `/abs/` page fetched first (title, single author, 15 pages / 5 figures, v1 2026-04-28, CC BY 4.0) then the **v1 HTML fetched clean on the first attempt** - 240,050 raw bytes, **46,217 chars** after tag strip, 1,183 lines, the shortest document in the recent head. `fetch.py` took a **bare name** (`2604.25143v1`). - **Done:** arxiv-2603.15678 - Spectral Edge Dynamics of Training Trajectories: Signal-Noise Geometry Across Scales (read full text v2: abstract, §1-§9 including the Discussion's window-artifact rebuttal and the Limitations paragraph, §2.1-§2.4 with Definitions 1-5 and Properties 1-6, §3.1-§3.7, §4.1-§4.6 with the residualised and multivariate Granger tables, §5, §6's cross-scale table and §7; all tables reconstructed from header columns and cross-checked against prose restatements; Figures 1-4 read from captions and the prose that reports them, not inspected as images; reference list read **in full** because five of its 26 entries are same-author 2026 preprints and the provenance matters)
- **Pattern:** **A trajectory statistic chosen as an argmax over candidates, and then validated by its correlation with the very target it is supposed to predict, is a fit and not a discovery - and this paper is the folder's clearest case because the winner changes with scale.** The signal/noise boundary is defined as the k maximising the ratio of consecutive singular values. At 51M that is sigma2/sigma3 (peak 1.747 ± 0.120 across four seeds) and it correlates with validation loss at |r| = 0.738. At 124M the paper ranks all five candidates by their loss correlation, and sigma2/sigma3 - the boundary that WAS the edge at 51M - comes **last** at 0.211, while sigma3/sigma4 wins at 0.870. The author then writes that "the correct spectral edge automatically emerges as the ratio most strongly coupled to training dynamics", which is selection on the outcome stated as if it were a property of the object. There is no a-priori rule for which ratio is the edge at a new scale. Mechanical rule for us: **any boundary, threshold or rank we define as the argmax of a ratio over a candidate family ships with the full candidate table, the rank of the winner against the outcome it explains, and an explicit note when that ranking chose it - and the word "emerged" is reserved for cases where the winner was fixed before the outcome was measured; otherwise we write "selected", and the selected statistic has to be cross-validated on held-out windows before it counts as predictive.** Cross-reference run 714 (spread-versus-integral ranking: the naive summary ranks the random-init collapse at the top while the integral ranks the real specialists - the same trap inside the model's own features), run 712 (a percentile threshold is stable where a maximum swings two orders of magnitude).
- **Pattern:** **When the null model is rejected by two orders of magnitude it cannot also be the detection floor in the same paper - and here the paper knows both things and reports both anyway.** Marchenko-Pastur predicts a noise-eigenvalue coefficient of variation of about 0.001 at this aspect ratio; the observed value is 0.688 at 51M and 0.26-0.50 at 124M, a factor of roughly 229-576. The author's own diagnosis is that Adam's second-moment preconditioner structures the noise, so the isotropic theory does not apply, and a coloured-noise generalisation is named as needed. Then Property 1 still declares the edge "detected" as a departure from that same isotropic prediction, and the MP edge is drawn on the spectrum plot. This folder has met the same structural error from the other direction (run 706: an inflated norm will usually reproduce an effect you attributed to structure), but this is the cleanest statement of it at the level of a *null* rather than a control. Mechanical rule for us: **an empirical detection claim is stated against a noise band measured from the same data (shuffled windows, held-out seeds, permuted time order), not against a theoretical null the same paper has shown to be wrong by orders of magnitude - because a structured-noise distribution has fat tails, and a ratio threshold calibrated on the isotropic prediction sits inside the noise.** Cross-reference run 706 (variance is not magnitude, norm-matched control), run 708 (a scale at initialisation decides what the fit finds later), run 713 (survey-sourced numbers are inherited, never re-measured - here the null is inherited and never re-measured either).
- **Pattern:** **A rolling-window monitor's claimed lead time is bounded by its own integration horizon, and Property 2 measures exactly that artefact before interpreting it as physics.** The lag between the edge ratio and validation loss flips from -1 to +2 as W grows from 10 to 30 (GPT-2), and the paper's interpretation is temporal integration - a larger window averages more history so it becomes a slower, leading summary. That interpretation is almost certainly right, and it is also the reason the companion grokking claim needs scrutiny: a window of W deltas at 200-step checkpoint spacing spans 200W steps, so at W = 10 the horizon is 2,000 steps, and the 1,700-step grokking lead reported from companion work is *shorter than the integration horizon of the statistic that produced it*. A slow moving average leading a fast series by roughly its own averaging length is a mathematical certainty, not evidence of a precursor. Mechanical rule for us: **every monitoring statistic we propose that is a rolling-window functional states its integration horizon in the same sentence as its lead time, and if the lead is comparable to or shorter than that horizon the result is reported as a level threshold rather than as a precursor - because the only thing that distinguishes a genuine early warning from an artefact of smoothing is a lead time that survives shortening the window.** Cross-reference run 712 (a between-checkpoint gap is not a transition; two transitions separated 10-20x in tokens), run 708 (a stability claim is worth more as a curve over a sweep than as a point).
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** The first carried candidate from run 715 - `arxiv-2603.15678`, the parameter-space companion of the run-714 spectral-probe methodology (Xu 2026c), gate-tested FREE in runs 710-715 and never taken - was **free** again, with all four checks printed before any fetch: `arxiv-2603.15678 aether:free core:free idx_a:0 idx_c:0`. - **Done:** arxiv-2604.14722 - A Mechanistic Account of Attention Sinks in GPT-2: One Circuit, Broader Implications for Mitigation (read full text v1: abstract, §1-§5 including all four limitation subsections, §3.1's score expansion and §3.2.1-§3.2.6 in full, Appendix A.1-A.3, Appendix B and B.1's norm-preserving swap construction; Table 1 reconstructed from its header column order and cross-checked against the ten prose bullets that follow it; Figures 1-8 read from captions and the prose that reports them, not inspected as images; reference list read only as far as resolving works cited in §1, §3.1 and Appendix B)
- **Pattern:** **The paper's own conclusion is the opposite of what its title suggests, and it is the folder's cleanest statement of "necessary in this model, dispensable in the family" - every one of the three circuit members is individually absent from an architecture that still has sinks.** Query biases (nullified here at 44.7% of baseline) are missing entirely from many reported architectures; MLPs can be dropped from the block (50.3% here) and sinks persist in MLP-free transformers; positional encodings can be removed (17.5% here) and sinks persist in models trained without them. The authors read this as optimisation reconstructing an equivalent sink from whatever components are available, so the parameter-level pathway is an *implementation*, not *the mechanism*. Mechanical rule for us: **when we identify the pathway carrying a behaviour, we report beside it every known architecture in which the behaviour persists without that pathway, and if any exist we write "one implementation" and never "the cause" - because a necessity result in one model and a dispensability result across a family are both facts, and reporting only the first reads a property of a draw as a property of the phenomenon.** Cross-reference run 714 (a circuit necessary in s42 and absent in s271, the same structure at seed granularity), run 713 (ablation damage is architecture-dependent), run 705 (collapsing a selector reveals which half was the mechanism).
- **Pattern:** **A control must be matched on the *axis of the claim*, not on size - and these ten interventions are the folder's most explicit three-axis matching.** Each specific intervention is paired with a control that spends the same budget on the same axis where the objection lives: three outlier key columns zeroed (65.2%) against three *random* key columns zeroed (100.0%), so the claim is about which columns and not how many parameters; replacing the first positional embedding (3.0%) against nullifying the BOS token at the same position (99.7%), so the claim is about position rather than token identity; swapping the MLP-processed positional direction (6.2%) against swapping the raw one with identical swap machinery (99.0%), so the claim is about the processed object rather than the act of swapping. Mechanical rule for us: **every ablation or steering claim we publish names the alternative explanation it defeats and exhibits a control that differs from the treatment on that axis alone, holding size, position and machinery fixed - because a control matched on parameter count but not on the axis where the doubt lives tests nothing, and the three pairs here are the template for how to pick the axis.** Cross-reference run 706 (the norm-matched control that turned a correlation into a mechanism), run 707 (magnitude and direction separated by three ratios), run 700 (a gain needs a null that spends the same budget).
- **Pattern:** **A mitigation that moves a statistic is not a mitigation that removes the phenomenon - this paper's best intervention relocates the sink instead of deleting it, and says so.** Replacing the first positional encoding takes BOS attention from 0.563 to 0.017, which reads as a near-total fix; but the authors' own framing is that the circuit *controls sink location*, so what actually happened is that attention moved to a different position, and the residual after every pathway intervention is reported as unexplained secondary contributors rather than as zero. Read against runs 705-711 this is the same lesson one level up: success scored at the original address alone is compatible with the phenomenon having moved. Mechanical rule for us: **every sink-suppression or feature-ablation result we report carries the statistic at the treated address *and* the maximum of that statistic at any other address, because a 3%-of-baseline reading is compatible both with a real fix and with a sink that relocated to position 2, and only the pair distinguishes them.** Cross-reference run 711 (gating deletes NOP sinks and multiplies broadcast sinks fortyfold), run 713 (ablation damage varies by family because each family answers "what else could carry this" differently), run 705 (removal versus relocation at the component level).

The gate was **re-run after the entry was written and before the INDEX append** (`aether:YES`, `core:free`, `idx_a:0`, `idx_c:0`) - the run-700 near-clobber rule held for the seventeenth consecutive run. **Discovery and tooling:** the experiment began with the `/html/2604.14722v1` URL directly (no `/abs/` pre-fetch - the abstract, author list, affiliation and the CC-BY licence line are all in the HTML body), which fetched **clean on the first attempt**: 44,594 clean chars after tag strip, 786 lines, the shortest document in the recent head (runs 711-714 were 75k-125k) because it is a 16-page single-model paper with no proof appendix. `fetch.py` took a **bare name** (`14722`). - **Done:** arxiv-2605.24059 - Spectral Probe-Circuits: A Three-Step Recipe for Identifying Attention-Head Circuits in Pretrained Transformers (read full text v2: abstract, §1-§9 including the limitations list and the practical recommendation, Appendix A in full, and §3.5's threshold-calibration tables and §5.3's nine-signal comparison; Tables 1-14 as cells cross-checked against prose restatements; Figures 1-4 read from captions and the prose that reports them, not inspected as images; reference list read only as far as resolving the works cited in §§1-2)
- **Pattern:** **A trajectory feature must be an integral, not a peak and not a spread - and this entry is the sharpest demonstration in the folder of why, because the artifact it defeats is one the folder has been treating as a finding.** Pythia's layer-0 heads begin at a participation ratio near 60 (random attention spreads mass across all positions, so the effective rank is high at initialisation) and collapse to 2-30 by the end of training, so ranking heads by spread - max minus min - puts exactly those collapsing heads at the top; ranking by the time integral of the clipped positive part demotes them and surfaces heads that *gain* sustained rank. The head-to-head table makes the size of the error visible: precision-at-30 is 0.93 for spread against 0.97 for the integral, but mean selectivity in the top five is 155x against 5,791x. Note what this does to every entry in this folder that read a dominant direction or a high rank out of a final checkpoint: a high effective rank is the *default* state of a random head, so the interesting quantity is not how high the rank is but how long it stayed high relative to where it started. Mechanical rule for us: **every trajectory-derived feature we define is specified as a functional of the whole curve - monotone in sustained behaviour, clipped or baseline-subtracted against its value at initialisation, and normalised by fraction of training rather than raw step count - and we print the spread-based and peak-based alternatives beside it, because on this panel the naive alternatives rank the random-init collapse as the top result while the integral ranks the actual specialists.** Cross-reference run 712 (a percentile threshold is stable while the maximum swings two orders of magnitude - the same lesson, one statistic upstream), run 708 (a scale at initialisation decides what the fit finds later).
- **Pattern:** **A circuit can be causally necessary in one seed and causally irrelevant in another trained on identical data with identical hyperparameters - the six-seed cross-ablation matrix is the cleanest negative in the folder and it constrains every "the circuit for X" claim we will ever make.** s42 implements the retrieval task with four layer-0 heads; s271 uses L6H{1,10} plus L7H{9,15} with no layer-0 head above a spread of 11. Ablating s271's circuit on s42 changes probe accuracy by nothing at any checkpoint (0.982 / 0.838 / 0.995 against baselines 0.982 / 0.843 / 0.995), and removing all 32 heads in L6+L7 from s42 still leaves 0.94 - so s271's implementation is not merely redundant on s42, it is absent. The reverse direction is not symmetric (s42's heads do hurt s271), and the seed with the broader late-layer circuit generalises better out of distribution. Note the mechanism the author draws from it: s42's circuit is an *instance* and s271's is a *class*. Mechanical rule for us: **no circuit or feature set is reported as "the" implementation of a capability without a same-architecture, different-seed ablation matrix, and where only one run exists we write "one implementation observed" rather than the definite article - because a causal necessity result on a single seed is a statement about one draw from a distribution over implementations, and this panel measured that distribution and found it spans layer 0 to layer 7.** Cross-reference run 712 (the formation circuit and the final circuit share only a third of their heads), run 704 (the head that fires on apostrophes and is otherwise silent).
- **Pattern:** **Screening quality and causal adequacy are different axes, and the gap between them is filled by distribution rather than dilution - the 43% / 77% / 100% ladder on one model is the measurement.** On Pythia 410M, top-6 spectral picks drop induction by 43%, a mech-interp-plus-second-class set by 77%, and the all-head screen at 50x by 100% with eleven heads, only three of which were in the spectral top-30. The unattractive explanation (dilution: we captured less of the circuit) and the attractive one (distribution: the circuit is spread over more heads at lower per-head selectivity) are separated by the fact that the full set of induction-selective heads exists and restores the whole effect. Mechanical rule for us: **when a screened set recovers only part of a causal effect we do not report the shortfall as noise or as a smaller mechanism - we run the exhaustive screen at a permissive threshold and check whether the missing effect is spread across heads the ranked screen did not surface, because a pre-filter's recall is bounded by the ranking it uses and the residual effect is exactly where that bound bites; and any circuit-size claim we publish carries its ablation-floor curve across thresholds, not a single point.** Cross-reference run 696 (a per-unit worth is a property of the ablated set), run 712 (a head that stopped showing the screened pattern was still inside the circuit).
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** The carried candidate from run 713 was the author's own companion methodology paper, named there only as `[ Xu, 2026d ]` with the instruction that its id be resolved from the reference list before any id was written down; this run did exactly that first: grep of `/tmp/lit/02378.txt` for the Xu entries returned four, and `Xu (2026d)` is *Spectral probe-circuits* with the arXiv id **2605.24059** (the other three are 2604.25143, an unnumbered composed-task companion, and 2603.15678). The dedupe gate (`/tmp/lit/gate.sh`) printed all four checks before any fetch: `arxiv-2605.24059 aether:free core:free idx_a:0 idx_c:0`, and the same for `arxiv-2603.15678` and `arxiv-2604.25143`. The gate was **re-run after the entry was written and before the INDEX append** (`aether:YES`, `core:free`, `idx_a:0`, `idx_c:0`) - the run-700 near-clobber rule held for the sixteenth consecutive run. **Discovery and tooling:** the `/abs/` page fetched first (42,501 raw / 5,109 clean chars: title, single author, v1 2026-05-22 and v2 2026-06-03, full abstract, DOI, the public repository URL), then the **v2 HTML fetched clean on the first attempt** - 396,966 raw bytes, **75,903 chars** after tag strip, http 200. `fetch.py` took a **bare name** (`24059`). - **Pattern:** **The survey's one structural claim worth carrying is that its eleven accounts are arranged by the *kind* of thing each explains, not by which is right - and that arrangement is the audit this folder has been missing.** The five levels are mathematical origin (softmax no-op; structural bias from causal masking and RoPE), training dynamics (active-dormant head reinforcement - the only account placed on that level), numerical mechanism (outlier circuits; outlier-driven rescaling; mix-compress-refine), geometric structure (geometric anchoring; anti-overmixing; spectral-energy association), and functional role (implicit attention bias). Placed against entries 704-712, this folder holds an independent reading of nearly every one of them, which converts the survey from a source of new mechanism into a **coverage and redundancy check**: the levels with only one account each in the whole literature are training dynamics and functional role, and those are exactly the levels where our own predictive ambitions sit. Mechanical rule for us: **whenever we add an account of a mechanism, we record which level it operates on and which other accounts occupy that level, and an account that is the sole occupant of its level is treated as untested rather than as established - because "no competitor" in this literature means "no one has built one", not "it survived".** Cross-reference run 704 (path-product bound: a mathematical-origin-level account), run 706 (link-by-link weight trace: numerical-mechanism level), run 710/711 (training-dynamics level, where every entry had to import its own dynamics experiment).
- **Pattern:** **Survey evidence is second-hand, and this document shows how second-hand it is at the level of the single number that the whole no-op account rests on.** The two classified evidence it marshals for the no-op family are observational (sink tokens have small value states, across BERT, ViT, LLaMA, OLMo and KV-quantization work) and causal (relax the sum-to-one constraint and the sink goes away - gating, clipped softmax, a `+1` denominator, an unnormalized rectified softmax, plain sigmoid), and every magnitude it quotes is inherited from the cited paper rather than re-measured; the survey's own study count is given as "over 210", "over 200" and "over 180" in three different sections. Note what this does to the *causal* half specifically: it is exactly the move runs 705-711 showed can be a relocation rather than a removal. Mechanical rule for us: **no survey-sourced number enters our own writing as a measurement - it enters as a pointer to the primary paper, and any account we adopt on survey authority alone is marked unverified until we have read the cited primary source; and where a survey's own internal counts disagree, that disagreement is recorded rather than averaged.** Cross-reference run 708 (a stability claim worth more as a curve than as a point), run 711 (mitigation success scored as a relocation), run 706 (the norm-matched control that made a correlation into a mechanism).
- **Pattern:** **Whether a sink "matters" is an architecture-level fact, not a sink-level one - and the survey's ablation table is the folder's widest cross-architecture sample of that.** ViTs degrade severely when their sink tokens are ablated; diffusion transformers lose almost nothing in text alignment but shift fine-grained perceptual structure by roughly six times a random-masking control; diffusion language models barely degrade at all because their sinks move during generation instead of sitting at a fixed position; MoE LLMs collapse entirely when three of several thousand experts are pruned; and in DiTs the sink carries semantic scene information rather than nullity, so the null-token reading of the word does not survive contact with that family. Read together with runs 704-712 this means the folder's most portable finding is a *conditional*: a sink is load-bearing to the degree the model's computation has been routed through the address. Mechanical rule for us: **every claim that a component is causally necessary is reported with the architecture family it was measured in and the ablation's damage on the sub-task the mechanism is supposed to serve, and a single-family ablation is never generalized into a statement about sinks as such - because the same ablation that zeroes a benchmark in one family leaves another family's quality unchanged, and the difference is the architecture's answer to "what else could carry this".** Cross-reference run 704 (deleting the first token zeroes RULER at 4096 context in Gemma 7B while costing nothing in a model trained without a fixed beginning token), run 711 (gating deletes NOP sinks and multiplies broadcast sinks fortyfold - the same family-internal version of the same lesson).

- **Done:** arxiv-2606.02378 - When Do Attention Circuits Form? Developmental Trajectories of Capability and Attention-Sink Emergence Across Three 1B-Class Architectures (read full text v2: abstract, §1-§13 including related work and the full limitations section, all six findings with Tables 1-8, §8.1 null-calibration table, §11.1-§11.4 discussion including the exploratory activation-versus-weight spectral bridge and its Spearman values; Figures 1-3 read from captions and the prose that reports them, not inspected as images; reference list read only as far as resolving the works cited in §§1-2 and §11.3)
- **Pattern:** **A negative result about where a mechanism can live is cheaper and sharper than a positive result about what it is - the L0/L1 zero-BOS floor is now a falsification test for any account in this cluster.** Zero heads classify as first-token in layers 0 and 1 at every one of the 30 sampled revisions across two corpora and two architecture families, from random initialisation to trillions of tokens, so the phenomenon every entry from 704 onward described at a final checkpoint is bounded by an identity that holds throughout training. Note what this does to the folder: a mechanistic theory that predicts sinks from residual-stream statistics must also predict that the two shallowest layers are off-limits, and the author's own qualitative reading - that position 0's embedding is a fixed function of one token, so attending there discards content until depth has mixed positions - is exactly the kind of claim our white-box model should be forced to make explicitly. Mechanical rule for us: **for every mechanism we claim, we also state where in the stack it must NOT appear and verify that at every checkpoint we have, because a prediction that a component can be anywhere is unfalsifiable, and a floor that holds across 30 runs and two corpora is one of the few identity-like statements this literature has produced.** Cross-reference run 704 (removing the first token breaks the model in exactly the sub-task the mechanism serves), run 710 (the sink's position is chosen by the data pipeline, not the architecture - here the layer position is chosen by the architecture and cannot be moved by the pipeline).
- **Pattern:** **Circuit membership is not the circuit - the same capability is implemented by a different set of heads as training proceeds, and what happens to a decayed head decides whether it was retired or moved.** Formation-time and final induction circuits share only about a third of their heads (Jaccard 0.29-0.33, stable across screen thresholds), and the same group ablation gives opposite readings depending on fate: heads that decayed into sinks go inert (Pythia, +0.3 points, z = +1.0) while heads that slid into the duplicate-token role stay causal (OLMoE L6·H1, -3.1 points, z = -5.5), and a head that was never an induction head at all but only a duplicate detector is causally necessary for induction (L3·H15, -3.2 against -0.48 for matched-random controls). The cleanest part is the within-model comparison: OLMoE's own sink-fate decayer is inert and its own duplicate-fate decayers are causal, so the "dense turns over, MoE accretes" contrast is an aggregate artefact of which fate the heads happened to take. Mechanical rule for us: **any circuit or feature set we identify at one point in a process is reported with its overlap against the same identification at another point, plus a fate for every element that dropped out - because a screening criterion measures the pattern it screens for, and a unit that stops showing that pattern may have moved inside the circuit rather than left it; the second case is invisible to the screen and shows up only as an ablation that still bites.** Cross-reference run 704 (a no-op write is a learned else-branch, not an inactive unit), run 711 (a signature vanishing without showing where the computation went is a relocation, not a fix).
- **Pattern:** **Two quantities that look like one statistic are not interchangeable, and the developmental panel is what separates them - a percentile threshold is stable across training while a maximum swings by two orders of magnitude.** Null-selectivity p99 holds in a 1.45-2.08x band across 140K steps, so a threshold calibrated once at the final checkpoint applies throughout; null-selectivity max wanders from 7.3 to 767 over the same revisions because it is set by isolated pathological draws (a concentrated head whose preferred target happens to align with a random null position). The same lesson appears in the headline result from the other side: the induction transition and the sink transition look like one event at a single checkpoint and separate by 10-20x in tokens once both are tracked across revisions (11.5x on OLMo, 21x on OLMoE), and the same DCLM corpus produces a sharp jump under dense OLMo and a gradual ramp under MoE OLMoE. Mechanical rule for us: **whenever our read-out depends on a threshold or a summary of a distribution, we state which functional of that distribution it is and check its stability across the checkpoints or seeds we have, and we never compare a curve's shape between two models unless the sampling grid is identical - because a maximum is not a threshold, a between-checkpoint gap is not a transition, and a one-checkpoint comparison cannot tell one transition from two.** Cross-reference run 710 (a scalar regulariser swings a sink statistic threefold), run 705 (the same gate reads as flat or selective depending on which normalisation the statistic lives in).

- **Done:** arxiv-2606.08105 - A Unifying View of Attention Sinks: From Mechanisms to Architectural Interventions (read full text v2: abstract, §1-§6 including discussion, Appendix A.1-A.5 controlled-task setups and causal-validation tables, Appendices B.1-B.4 with all four lemma statements and proofs, C.1-C.3 recipes and probe protocols, D register results, E.1-E.3 relocation and penalty ablations; Tables 1, 2, 3, 8, 9, 14 as cells cross-checked against prose; Figures 1-18 read from captions and the prose that reports them, not inspected as images)
- **Pattern:** **The same observable is compatible with two opposite computations, and the cheap discriminator is not the observable but the norm and the rank of what the attended position writes - this is the entry that finally gives the folder a scale on which its own sink statistics are ambiguous.** Every entry from 704 onward measured concentration, and concentration separates nothing: a NOP sink (near-zero value) and a broadcast sink (content-scale value, rank-one update) both produce a stripe, are both present in the same pretrained DINOv2-G, and both pass any sink-fraction count. The two modes are so distinguishable that a two-number projection of the same model splits them cleanly, but only once the value norm ratio and the update rank are added to the attention matrix. Mechanical rule for us: **no attention-concentration statistic is reported alone - it ships as a triple (fraction of attention mass on the destination, norm of what that destination writes relative to the head's ordinary values, rank of the resulting update), because a near-zero write and a rank-one shared write are identical in every pure-attention view we have been taking, and the write is what the residual stream actually receives.** Cross-reference run 705 (the same gate reading as flat or selective depending on which normalisation the statistic lives in), run 710 (the sink-fraction statistic swung threefold by weight decay alone - a statistic with no mechanism attached).
- **Pattern:** **Interventions must be split by what they leave intact, and this paper is the cleanest statement in the folder of the difference between touching the payload and touching the routing.** Value patching holds the learned attention matrix fixed, so it asks what information sits at the destination; removing the sink column and renormalising asks whether routing through the destination was necessary at all, and everything downstream is destroyed with it - the authors label the second a necessity test and refuse to read it as selective removal. The payoff is a case where the two disagree: in the controlled broadcast model the layer-one sink is causally necessary by every routing and zero-ablation measure, yet donor patching there transfers almost nothing (payload alignment 0.162 against 0.944 at layer two), so "this sink matters" and "this sink is the channel" come apart in the same model. Mechanical rule for us: **when we patch or ablate a component we state which of the three things the intervention preserves - the direction of the write, the routing to it, or neither - and we do not infer that a component carries a signal from the fact that deleting it hurts, because a necessary relay that carries nothing transferable fails exactly that way.** Cross-reference run 707 (a radial map designed so the forward and backward effects separate by construction), run 705 (a bound tested with the upstream gradients held fixed).
- **Pattern:** **An intervention that removes a mechanism does not remove the computation - the model routes it somewhere else, and the somewhere else is measurable.** Gating deletes detected NOP sinks outright (0.25 to 0.00 per image) and broadcast sinks multiply from 0.09 to 3.80, forty times over; registers absorb most sink mass and then divide into the same two phenotypes; the penalised global pathway was designed as a place for the shared computation to live and it takes the causal load off attention entirely in the controlled task (removing the attention update leaves task MSE at 0.3648, removing the global pathway raises it to 1.6539). And the variant that suppresses broadcast most effectively is the one that performs worst on dense probes, so the mitigation leaderboard and the representation-quality leaderboard are anti-correlated. Mechanical rule for us: **every architectural or training intervention we propose is scored on the full set of behaviours it could have displaced, plus downstream quality, and an experiment that shows a signature vanishing without showing where the computation went is reported as a relocation, not a fix - because an intervention that removes a sink can convert one null write into forty shared writes, and the score we designed the intervention to improve will not see that.** Cross-reference run 704 (removing the first token breaks the model in exactly the sub-task the mechanism serves), run 709 (a gate that survives its own routing being deleted is the gate that was the mechanism).
- **Could not read:** nothing failed twice and nothing was abandoned. **Selection and gate.** The first carried candidate from run 710 - `arxiv-2606.08105`, gate-tested FREE there and never taken - was **free** again, and the dedupe gate printed all five checks in one command (`/tmp/lit/gate.sh`) before any fetch: `arxiv-2606.08105 aether:free core:free idx_a:0 idx_c:0`, `arxiv-2606.02378 aether:free core:free idx_a:0 idx_c:0`, `arxiv-2604.10098 aether:free core:free idx_a:0 idx_c:0`, plus a lowercased title-substring count (`unifying view of attention sinks`) of **0** on both INDEX files. - **Done:** arxiv-2410.10781 - When Attention Sink Emerges in Language Models: An Empirical View (read full text v2: abstract, §1-§3 with Tables 1-3, §4-§7 with all takeaway summaries, §8 future work, Appendix C.1 Propositions 1-4 with proofs, Appendix B formulations and Appendix D/E located by heading; Tables 1-6 as cells cross-checked against prose; Figures 2-8 read from captions and the prose that reports them, not inspected as images; no limitations section exists in this paper, so the Limits section quotes its future-work paragraph instead)
- **Pattern:** **The sink is not a property of the architecture - the same recipe moves it by an order of magnitude on weight decay alone, and the data pipeline chooses its address.** Two runs with identical architecture differ from 15.20 to 41.08 on nothing but the weight-decay ratio, and the position of the sink follows the packing pipeline rather than the model: randomise the first token of each chunk and the fraction goes *up* to 27.03; randomise the first two and the sink moves to position two (14.08 there against 1.98 at position one); fix a designated token at position two or three and the sink sits there. Note what this does to the folder: every entry from 704 onward that reads a sink out of a released checkpoint is, on this evidence, reading a handful of undocumented pre-training decisions as much as a mechanism. Mechanical rule for us: **whenever we attribute a read-out to a head's learned behaviour, we state the pre-training or fine-tuning pipeline that produced the model in the same sentence, and when the pipeline is undocumented we say so and downgrade the claim to a description of that checkpoint - because trajectories that differ in the pipeline are not two samples of one phenomenon, and a statistic that a single scalar regulariser swings threefold cannot be a stable unit of analysis.** Cross-reference run 709 (a gate that survives its own routing being deleted is the gate), run 707 (a scale at initialisation, invisible in final accuracy - the same lesson about where a difference in outcome really came from).
- **Pattern:** **The paper manufactures the mechanism as an explicit parameter and then varies its magnitude continuously - the bias ladder is the cheapest causal instrument in this cluster and the value-bias sweep is its best result.** Key bias alone (value bias fixed at zero) relocates the sink off the first token while validation loss stays at 3.72, so the key-side score is doing the work and the value-side write is decoration. The sweep then shows the reverse direction: raise the length of the fixed value bias and attention migrates *back* from the bias to the first token (sink on the bias 73.34 -> 1.51 at twenty times the unit vector's length, with the first-token sink rising to 25.88), because a large value write is expensive to cancel inside the residual stream, so the optimiser re-points the keys of token one to absorb the same surplus. Mechanical rule for us: **when we claim a component supplies a function, we instantiate that function as an explicit parameter at the same position, hold the rest of the architecture fixed, and sweep the parameter through at least three magnitudes - because a knob that switches a behaviour on, off, and back off again at the far end is a claim about the fitted solution rather than about the component, and the far end is where the reason shows itself.** Cross-reference run 705 (a mechanism versus a scale knob separated by replacing selection with its average), run 708 (a derived parameter reported beside the swept optimum).
